\frac{dz_{1}}{z_{1}}\wedge\frac{dz_{2}}{z_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\frac{dz_{k}}{z_{k}}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}\otimes z_{K}.

We shall prove that, if
\overline{K}
is the complement of
K
in
I

P_{R}S_{r}(\alpha,\overline{\alpha})=\frac{(-1)^{r}}{(r+1)!}\tau^{\overline{K}}_{+}S_{Y^{\overline{K}}}(\phi_{r}\alpha,\overline{\phi_{r}\alpha}).

Without loss of generality, we can assume that
K=\{r+1,r+2,\dots,k\}
. Let
\partial_{\bar{K}}=\prod^{r}_{i=0}\partial_{i}
. Then

\displaystyle P_{R}S_{r}(\alpha,\overline{\alpha})=

\displaystyle={\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{s=0}(-s)^{r}\int_{X}|z_{\overline{K}}|^{2s-2}\prod^{k}_{j=r+1}|z_{j}|^{2s}\eta\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge{d\overline{z_{i}}})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r}{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{s=0}s^{-(r+2)}\int_{X}\prod^{k}_{i=0}|z_{i}|^{2s}\partial_{\bar{K}}\overline{\partial_{\bar{K}}}(\eta)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge{d\overline{z_{i}}}),

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{r}}{(r+1)!}\int_{X}\left(\log\prod^{k}_{i=0}|z_{i}|^{2}\right)^{r+1}\partial_{\bar{K}}\overline{\partial_{\bar{K}}}(\eta)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge{d\overline{z_{i}}})

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{r}}{(r+1)!}\int_{X}\prod^{r}_{i=0}\log|z_{i}|^{2}\partial_{\bar{K}}\overline{\partial_{\bar{K}}}(\eta)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge{d\overline{z_{i}}})\quad(\star)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{r}}{(r+1)!}\int_{Y^{\overline{K}}}\eta\bigwedge^{n}_{i=r+1}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge{d\overline{z_{i}}})

\displaystyle\text{(Poincar\'{e}-Lelong equation~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GH}{}{}, Page 388]})}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{r}}{(r+1)!}\tau^{{\overline{K}}}_{+}S_{Y^{\overline{K}}}({\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{Y^{\overline{K}}}\frac{dt}{t}\wedge\alpha,\overline{{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{Y^{\overline{K}}}\frac{dt}{t}\wedge\alpha}).

The equality
(\star)
holds because if we expand
\left(\log\prod^{k}_{i=0}|z_{i}|^{2}\right)^{r+1}
as a linear combination of
\prod^{k}_{i=0}\left(\log|z_{i}|^{2}\right)^{a_{i}}
with
\sum^{k}_{i=0}a_{i}=r+1
, the only possible non-exact form among

\prod^{k}_{i=0}\left(\log|z_{i}|^{2}\right)^{a_{i}}\partial_{\bar{K}}\overline{\partial_{\bar{K}}}(\eta)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge{d\overline{z_{i}}}),

is
\left(\prod^{r}_{i=0}\log|z_{i}|^{2}\right)\partial_{\bar{K}}\overline{\partial_{\bar{K}}}(\eta)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge{d\overline{z_{i}}})
. Note that while
{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{Y^{\overline{K}}}
depends on the order of the index sets
K
and
I
, the pairing

\frac{(-1)^{r}}{(r+1)!}\tau^{(r+1)}_{+}S_{{\tilde{Y}^{(r+1)}}}(\phi_{r}\alpha,\overline{\phi_{r}\beta})=\frac{(-1)^{r}}{(r+1)!}\tau^{{\overline{K}}}_{+}S_{Y^{\overline{K}}}({\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{Y^{\overline{K}}}\frac{dt}{t}\wedge\alpha,\overline{{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{Y^{\overline{K}}}\frac{dt}{t}\wedge\alpha})

does not because the sign cancels out. We complete the proof.
∎

6.2.
Construction of the limiting mixed Hodge structure

We are going to show that the triple
\left({\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}},F,W\right)
gives a mixed Hodge complex. Unlike the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-mixed Hodge complex considered by Deligne
[
Del71
]
, where the rational structure is a required input, we do not have this piece of information in our situation. We will redo Deligne’s argument on mixed Hodge complex by sesquilinear pairings. It is also worth noting that the sesquilinear pairing simplifies the process of verifying that the first page weight spectral sequence of
{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}
is a polarized bigraded Hodge-Lefschetz structure, making it easier than the situation in
[
GNA90
]
, where they need to decompose the differential
d_{1}
on the first page into a combinatorial differential and a sum of Gysin morphisms.

We first set up the pairing on each page of the weight spectral sequence abstractly. Let
{\mathcal{N}}
be a holonomic
{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}
-module with compact support equipped with a sesquilinear pairing
S\colon{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\mathcal{N}}}\to\mathfrak{C}_{Z}
on a complex manifold
Z
. Let
N
be a nilpotent operator on
{\mathcal{N}}
such that
S\circ({\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{d}\,}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{N})=S\circ(N\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{d}\,})
. Let
W_{\bullet}{\mathcal{N}}
be the monodromy filtration associated to
N
on
{\mathcal{N}}
. Let
E^{i,j}_{r}
be the weight spectral sequence convergent to
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-i}{\mathbf{H}}^{i+j}(Z,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z}{\mathcal{N}})
with
E^{i,j}_{1}={\mathbf{H}}^{i+j}(Z,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-i}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z}{\mathcal{N}})
. By abuse of notation, denote by
S_{k}=\varepsilon(k)S_{k}^{\prime}
where

\displaystyle S_{k}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\colon{\mathbf{H}}^{k}(Z,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z}{\mathcal{N}})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\mathbf{H}}^{-k}(Z,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z}{\mathcal{N}})}\to

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{H}}^{0}(Z,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z,\overline{Z}}{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\mathcal{N}}})\to{\mathbf{H}}^{0}_{c}(Z,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z,\overline{Z}}\mathfrak{C}_{Z})\simeq{\mathbb{C}}.

Let
a
be a local section of
\left({\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z}{\mathcal{N}}\right)^{-j-1}
and
b
be a local section of
\left({\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z}{\mathcal{N}}\right)^{i}
. Then

D(a\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{b})=da\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{b}+(-1)^{-j-1}a\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{db}

for
D
a differential on
{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z,\overline{Z}}{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\mathcal{N}}}
. Applying
S
, we find that

(6.24)

DS(a,b)=S(da,b)+(-1)^{-j-1}S(a,\overline{db}).

Since the differential
d
is compatible with the weight filtration, we have an induced pairing
E_{1}(S)_{k}=\varepsilon(k)E_{1}(S^{\prime})_{k}
on the first page
E^{i,j}_{1}
of the weight spectral sequence, where

\displaystyle E_{1}(S^{\prime})_{k}

\displaystyle\colon{\mathbf{H}}^{k}(Z,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-i}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z}{\mathcal{N}})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\mathbf{H}}^{-k}(Z,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{i}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z}{\mathcal{N}})}\to

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{H}}^{0}(Z,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z,\overline{Z}}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-i}{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{i}{\mathcal{N}}})\to{\mathbf{H}}^{0}(Z,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z,\overline{Z}}\mathfrak{C}_{Z})\simeq{\mathbb{C}}.

Then by equation (
6.24
) we obtain

0=\varepsilon(-j)E_{1}(S)_{-j}(d_{1}a,\bar{b})+\varepsilon(-j-1)(-1)^{-j-1}E_{1}(S)_{-j-1}(a,\overline{d_{1}b}),

due to
DS(a,\bar{b})
is cohomologous to zero. Working out the sign, we find that

E_{1}(S)_{-j}(d_{1}a,\bar{b})+E_{1}(S)_{-j-1}(a,\overline{d_{1}b})=0,

i.e. the differential
d_{1}
is skew-symmetric with respect to
E_{1}(S)
. It follows that we have an induced pairing on the second page:
E_{2}(S)_{k}:E^{i,k-i}_{2}\otimes\overline{E^{-i,-k+i}_{2}}\to{\mathbb{C}}
since
E_{2}=\ker d_{1}/\mathrm{Im\,}d_{1}
. It also follows from that the equation (
6.24
), the differential
d_{2}
is skew-symmetric with respect to
E_{2}(S)
. By an inductive argument, we get the induced pairing
E_{r}(S):E_{r}\otimes\overline{E_{r}}\to{\mathbb{C}}
on the
r
-th page of the weight spectral sequence
E_{r}\otimes\overline{E_{r}}\to{\mathbb{C}}
such that
d_{r}
is skew-symmetric with respect to
E_{r}(S)
for every
r\geq 1
.

Next, let
L=[\omega]\wedge
be a Lefschetz operator for a Kähler class
[\omega]\in H^{1}(Z,\Omega_{Z})\cap H^{2}(Z,{\mathbb{R}})
and
{\mathsf{X}}=2\pi\sqrt{-1}L
on
Z
. We can also regard
{\mathsf{X}}
as a morphism
{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z}{\mathcal{N}}\to{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z}{\mathcal{N}}[2]
. Let us work out the relation between the sesquilinear pairing
S_{k}
and the operator
{\mathsf{X}}
. By functoriality, we have the following commutative diagram in
{\mathbf{D}}^{b}(Z,{\mathbb{C}})
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z,\overline{Z}}{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\mathcal{N}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z,\overline{Z}}\mathfrak{C}_{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z,\overline{Z}}\mathfrak{Db}_{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}^{\bullet+2n}_{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{Db}_{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z,\overline{Z}}{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\mathcal{N}}}\left[2\right]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z,\overline{Z}}\mathfrak{C}_{Z}\left[2\right]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Z,\overline{Z}}\mathfrak{Db}_{Z}\left[2\right]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}^{\bullet+2n+2}_{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{Db}_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{d}\,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S[2]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Similarly, we have
S[2]\circ({\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{d}\,}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\mathsf{X}}})={\overline{{\mathsf{X}}}}S
. It follows from
{\mathsf{X}}+\overline{{\mathsf{X}}}=0
on
{\mathcal{A}}^{\bullet+2n}_{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{Db}
that

(6.25)

\varepsilon(k)S_{k}({\mathsf{X}}-,-)+\varepsilon(k-2)S_{k-2}(-,\bar{\mathsf{X}}-)=0,\quad\text{i.e. }S_{k}({\mathsf{X}}-,-)=S_{k-2}(-,\bar{\mathsf{X}}-).

Returning to our situation, we begin to construct a polarized bigraded Hodge-Lefschetz structure on

{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}{\mathbf{H}}^{\bullet}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}).

Fix a Kähler class
[\omega]
on
X
and let
L=[\omega]\wedge\colon{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\to{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}[2]
be the Lefschetz operator and
{\mathsf{X}}_{1}=2\pi\sqrt{-1}L
as the discussion above. Relabel the first page of the weight spectral sequence by

V_{\ell,k}={\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{k}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}})=\prescript{W}{}{E^{-k,\ell+k}_{1}}.

Let
V=\bigoplus_{\ell,k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}V_{\ell,k}
with filtration
F_{\bullet}V
induced by
F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}
. Denote by
E_{i}(R)
the induced operator by
R
on
\prescript{W}{}{E_{i}}
and let
{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}=E_{1}(R)
. Let
S_{\ell,k}=\varepsilon(\ell)S_{\ell,k}^{\prime}
for
\ell,k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
be the induced pairing on
V_{\ell,k}\otimes\overline{V_{-\ell,-k}}
where

\displaystyle S_{\ell,k}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\colon{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{k}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}})\otimes\overline{{\mathbf{H}}^{-\ell}(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-k}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}})\to

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{H}}^{0}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X,\overline{X}}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{k}{\mathcal{M}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-k}{\mathcal{M}}})\to{\mathbf{H}}^{0}_{c}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X,\overline{X}}\mathfrak{C}_{X})\simeq{\mathbb{C}}.

Let
d_{1}
be the differential of
E_{1}
. In terms of relabeling, we have

d_{1}\colon(V_{\ell,k},F_{\bullet}V_{\ell,k})\to(V_{\ell+1,k-1},F_{\bullet}V_{\ell+1,k-1}).

Theorem 6.6
.

The tuple
(V,{\mathsf{X}}_{1},{\mathsf{Y}}_{2},F_{\bullet}V,\bigoplus S_{j,k})
is a differential polarized bigraded Hodge-Lefschetz structure of central weight
n
and
d_{1}
is a differential of the bigraded Hodge-Lefschetz structure.

Proof.

Let us first check the conditions in Theorem
2.8
one by one. It is clear that two operators
{\mathsf{X}}_{1},{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}
are commute. Moreover, we have
{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}\colon(V_{\ell,k},F_{\bullet}V_{\ell,k})\to(V_{\ell,k-2},F_{\bullet+1}V_{\ell,k-2})
such that

{\mathsf{Y}}^{k}_{2}\colon F_{\bullet}V_{\ell,k}\to F_{\bullet+k}V_{\ell,-k},

is an isomorphism by Theorem
5.6
. Denote by
P_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}}V_{-j,r}
the
{{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}}
-primitive part
\ker{{\mathsf{Y}}^{r+1}_{2}}\cap V_{-j,r}={\mathbf{H}}^{-j}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{P}}_{r})
. It follows from Theorem
5.7
that

\phi_{r}\colon(P_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}}V_{-j,r},F_{\bullet}P_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}}V_{-j,r})\simeq H^{-j}\left(\tilde{Y}^{(r+1)},{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{\tilde{Y}^{(r+1)}}\omega_{{\tilde{Y}^{(r+1)}}}\right)(-r).

Therefore,
{\mathsf{X}}_{1}\colon F_{\bullet}P_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}}V_{-j,r}\to F_{\bullet-1}P_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}}V_{-j+2,r}
and by Hard Lefschetz,

{\mathsf{X}}^{j}_{1}\colon F_{\bullet}P_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}}V_{-j,r}\to F_{\bullet-j}P_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}}V_{j,r}

is an isomorphism. It follows from the Lefschetz decomposition of
{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}
that

{\mathsf{X}}^{j}_{1}\colon F_{\bullet}V_{-j,r}\to F_{\bullet-j}V_{j,r}

is an isomorphism. This proves
(\mathrm{pbHL1})
in Theorem
2.8
. And (pbHL2) for
{\mathsf{X}}_{1}
follows from the equation (
6.25
).

Because the operator
R
self-adjoint with respect to
S
by Corollary
6.4
, we have
S_{j,r}(-,\overline{{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}}-)=S_{j,r+2}({\mathsf{Y}}_{2}-,-)
. By Theorem
6.5
, the morphism
\phi_{r}
identifies
P_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}}S_{-j,r}\colon=S_{-j,r}(-,\overline{{\mathsf{Y}}^{r}_{2}}-)
with
\frac{(-1)^{r}}{(r+1)!}S_{\tilde{Y}^{(r+1)},-j}
. Recall that

S_{\tilde{Y}^{(r+1)},j}(a,\bar{b})={\frac{\varepsilon(n-r+j+1)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{n-r}}}\int_{\tilde{Y}^{(r+1)}}a\wedge\bar{b},

for
a\in H^{n-r+j}(\tilde{Y}^{(r+1)})
and
b\in H^{n-r-j}(\tilde{Y}^{(r+1)})
, and that
S_{\tilde{Y}^{(r+1)},j}({\mathsf{X}}^{j}_{1}-,-)
is a polarization on
H^{n-r-j}_{\mathrm{prim}}(\tilde{Y}^{(r+1)},{\mathbb{C}})
. The bi-primitive part
P_{-j,r}=\ker{\mathsf{X}}^{j}_{1}\cap\ker{\mathsf{Y}}^{r}_{2}\cap V_{-j,r}
together with the induced filtration
F_{\bullet}P_{-j,r}
and the pairing
S_{j,r}({\mathsf{X}}^{j}_{1}-,(-\overline{{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}})^{r}-)
is identified with the polarized Hodge structure
H^{n-r-j}_{\mathrm{prim}}(\tilde{Y}^{(r+1)},{\mathbb{C}})(-r)
via
\phi_{r}
. This proves (pbHL3).

It remains to prove that
d_{1}
is a differential of the bigraded Hodge-Lefschetz structure
V
. Clearly, we have

[d_{1},{\mathsf{X}}_{1}]=[d_{1},{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}]=0

because
d_{1}
is induced by the differential of
{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}
and
d_{1}
preserves
F_{\bullet}
. The differential
d_{1}
is skew-symmetric with respect to
\bigoplus_{j,r}S_{j,r}
formally follows the discussion at the beginning of this subsection. Thus, we finished checking that
d_{1}
is a differential.
∎

Corollary 6.7
.

We have the following

(1)

the Hodge spectral sequence degenerates at
\prescript{}{F}{E_{1}}
,

(2)

the weight spectral sequence degenerates at
\prescript{W}{}{E_{2}}
,

(3)

The tuple
\left(\bigoplus_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}),F,{\mathsf{X}}_{1},{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}\right)
together with the pairing induced by
\bigoplus S_{j,k}
is a polarized bigraded Hodge-Lefschetz structure of central weight
n
.

Proof.

We slightly modify the idea of cohomological mixed Hodge complex in
[
Del71
]
for statements
(1)
and
(2)
. Let
V^{k}_{\ell,r}\colon=\prescript{W}{}{E_{k}}^{-r,\ell+r}
be the relabeling of the
k
-th page of the weight spectral sequence. I claim that
V^{k}_{\ell,r}
together with the induced filtration
F_{\bullet}
and the induced pairing
S^{k}_{\ell,r}\circ\left({\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{d}\,}\otimes\bar{\mathsf{w}}\right)\colon V^{k}_{\ell,r}\otimes\overline{V^{k}_{\ell,r}}\to{\mathbb{C}}
is a polarized Hodge structure of weight
n+\ell+r
and the differential
d_{k}\colon V^{k}_{\ell,r}\to V^{k}_{\ell+1,r-k}
is a morphism of Hodge structures. Indeed, the differential
d_{k}
is skew-symmetric with respect to the sesquilinear pairing, i.e.
S^{k}_{\ell,r}(d_{k}-,-)+S^{k}_{\ell+1,r-k}(-,\overline{d_{k}}-)=0
. Therefore, if
(-1)^{q}S^{k}_{\ell,r}\circ\left({\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{d}\,}\otimes\bar{\mathsf{w}}\right)
for
q=n+\ell+r-p
is a Hermitian inner product on

{\left(V^{k}_{\ell,r}\right)^{p,q}=\{a\in F^{p}V^{k}_{\ell,r}\colon S^{k}_{\ell,r}(a,\bar{b})=0\text{ for all }b\in F^{p-\ell-r+1}V^{k}_{-\ell,-r}\}}

then
(-1)^{q}S^{k+1}_{\ell,r}\circ\left({\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{d}\,}\otimes\overline{{\mathsf{w}}}\right)
is also a Hermitian inner product on

\left(V^{k+1}_{\ell,r}\right)^{p,q}=\{a\in F^{p}V^{k+1}_{\ell,r}\colon S^{k+1}_{\ell,r}(a,\bar{b})=0\text{ for all }b\in F^{p-\ell-r+1}V^{k+1}_{-\ell,-r}\}.

In particular, we have the decomposition

V^{k+1}_{\ell,r}=\bigoplus_{p+q=n+\ell+r}\left(V^{k+1}_{\ell,r}\right)^{p,q}

and the morphism
d_{k}\colon\left(V^{k}_{\ell,r}\right)^{p,q}\to\left(V^{k+1}_{\ell,r}\right)^{p,q}
is compatible with the decomposition. See Remark
2.9
. By induction, the claim is proved. It follows that
d_{k}
vanishes for
k\geq 2
by it is a morphism of Hodge structures of different weights, which proves
(2)
.

We turn to the proof of
(1)
. The two vector spaces
{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{r}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\right)
and
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}V_{\ell,r}
are isomorphic because the Hodge spectral sequence on
{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{r}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}})
degenerates at the first page. Specifically, we have

({\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{r}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}},F)\simeq\bigoplus_{k\geq 0,-\frac{r}{2}}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}(R^{k}{\mathcal{P}}_{r+2k},F)(k),

which is isomorphic to
\bigoplus_{k\geq 0,-\frac{r}{2}}\left(\Omega^{\bullet+n-r-2k}_{\tilde{Y}^{(r+2k+1)}},F\right)(k+r)
by Theorem
5.7
. Meanwhile, the Hodge spectral sequence on
{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(\tilde{Y}^{r+2k+1},\Omega^{\bullet+n-r-2k}_{\tilde{Y}^{(r+2k+1)}}\right)
degenerates at the first page by the usual Hodge theory on compact Kähler manifolds. Consider another weight spectral sequence:

V_{\ell,r}({\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathcal{M}})={\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{r}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\right)\Rightarrow{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\right)

Here, we use
V^{\bullet}_{\ell,r}({\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathcal{M}})=E_{\bullet}^{-r,\ell+r}({\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathcal{M}})
to denote the spectral sequences and set
V^{1}_{\ell,r}({\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathcal{M}})=V_{\ell,r}({\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathcal{M}})
. We have
V_{\ell,r}({\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathcal{M}})\simeq{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}V_{\ell,r}
and the differentials of the
E_{1}
-pages are compatible:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\ell,r}({\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathcal{M}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}V_{\ell,r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\ell+1,r-1}({\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathcal{M}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}V_{\ell+1,r-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

as
{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{r}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\right)\simeq{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{r}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\right)
, and

{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{r}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}={\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{r}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}},

This implies that we have the isomorphism on the second page as well:

V_{\ell,r}^{2}({\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathcal{M}})\simeq V^{2}_{\ell,r}={\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{r}{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\right).

It follows that

\displaystyle\dim{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\right)\leq\dim\bigoplus_{r}V_{\ell,r}^{2}({\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathcal{M}})

\displaystyle=\dim\bigoplus_{r}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{r}{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\right)=\dim{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\right).

On the other hand, considering the Hodge spectral sequence, we also have

\dim{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\right)\leq\dim{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\right),

which forces that
\dim{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\right)=\dim{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\right)
and therefore,

{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\right)\simeq{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}\right).

This proves that the Hodge spectral sequence on
{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}})
degenerates at
\prescript{}{F}{E_{1}}
.

The statement
(3)
follows from Theorem
2.10
.
∎

The third statement in the above corollary ensures that the weight filtration on
{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}})
is the monodromy weight filtration of the nilpotent operator
R
, i.e.
R\colon W_{\bullet}{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}})\to W_{\bullet-2}{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}})(-1)

and
R^{r}\colon{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{r}{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}})\to{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-r}{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}})(-r)
is a filtered isomorphism. We have proved Theorem
A
for the case when
Y
is reduced.

7.
Non-reduced case: Generalized eigenspace
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
and the weight filtration

Now we move to the general situation. Let
I
be the index set consisting of indices of irreducible components of
Y
and
e_{i}
is the multiplicity of
Y
along the component
Y_{i}
.

7.1.
The generalized eigen-modules
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}

We start by studying the generalized eigen-modules
\ker(R-\alpha)^{\infty}
of the morphism
R
in the category of filtered
{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
-modules. These generalized eigen-modules naturally forms submodules of
{\mathcal{M}}
but the induced filtration on does not align with the expected weight of the mixed Hodge structure and is difficult to calculate. Instead, we adopt the idea of Saito in
[
Sai90
]
: we consider the generalized eigen-module as a sub-quotient of
{\mathcal{M}}
and impose the induced filtration on it. It turns out this filtration has desirable properties.

We proceed to introduce some notation. Let

{\mathcal{M}}_{\geq\alpha}=\ker\left(\prod_{\lambda\geq\alpha}(R-\lambda)^{\infty}\right),\quad{\mathcal{M}}_{>\alpha}=\ker\left(\prod_{\lambda>\alpha}(R-\lambda)^{\infty}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}={\mathcal{M}}_{\geq\alpha}/{\mathcal{M}}_{>\alpha}.

Then
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
is canonically isomorphic to the generalized eigen-module
\ker\left(R-\alpha\right)^{\infty}
. We equip
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
with the filtration
F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
induced from
({\mathcal{M}},F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}})
given by

F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}=\frac{{\mathcal{M}}_{\geq\alpha}\cap F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}}{{\mathcal{M}}_{>\alpha}\cap F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}}.

There are parallel definitions of the relative log de Rham complex. Denote by
C^{\bullet}=\Omega^{\bullet+n}_{X/\Delta}(\log Y)\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{Y}
for simplicity. Define sub-complexes of
C^{\bullet}
by

C^{\bullet}_{\geq\alpha}=C^{\bullet}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-\left\lceil\alpha Y\right\rceil),\quad C^{\bullet}_{>\alpha}=C^{\bullet}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-\left\lfloor\alpha Y\right\rfloor-Y_{{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{d}}})\quad\text{ and }C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}=C^{\bullet}_{\geq\alpha}/C^{\bullet}_{>\alpha},

where
Y_{{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{d}}}
is the associated reduced divisor of
Y
. If we let
I_{\alpha}
be the subset of
I
consisting of all
i
such that
\alpha e_{i}
is an integer, then

C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}=C^{\bullet}_{\geq\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{Y_{I_{\alpha}}},\quad\text{where }Y_{I_{\alpha}}=\sum_{i\in I_{\alpha}}Y_{i}.

One can check
C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}
is a generalized eigen-perverse sheaves of the residue
[\nabla]
. Since
{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)
is preserved by relative log differential
{\mathscr{T}}_{X/\Delta}(-\log Y)
, the multiplication by relative log differentials gives a morphism, recalling that
D_{1},D_{2},\dots,D_{n}
are local generators of
{\mathscr{T}}_{X/\Delta}(-\log Y)
dual to the local generators
\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\dots,\xi_{n}
of
\Omega_{X/\Delta}(\log Y)
,

(7.26)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}

\displaystyle\to\Omega_{X/\Delta}(\log Y)(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X},

\displaystyle z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}\otimes P

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{j}\xi_{j}\otimes D_{j}z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}\otimes P=\sum_{j}\xi_{j}\otimes z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}(D_{j}+\alpha_{j})\otimes P,

where, using the multi-index notation,
z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}=\prod_{i\in I}z^{\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil}_{i}
denotes the local generator of
{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)
and define
\alpha_{i}=[D_{i},z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}]/{z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}}={\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil}/{e_{i}}-{\lceil\alpha e_{0}\rceil}/{e_{0}}.
The morphism extends to a complex
\Omega^{{}n+\bullet}_{X/\Delta}(\log Y)(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
, which is a subcomplex of
\Omega^{{}n+\bullet}_{X/\Delta}(\log Y)\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
(see (
4.16
)). Tensoring
{\mathscr{O}}_{Y}
on the left gives
C^{\bullet}_{\geq\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
by the above definition. Further tensoring
{\mathscr{O}}_{Y_{I_{\alpha}}}
on the left, we obtain the complex of induced
{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
-modules
C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
with the filtration defined by

F_{\ell}\left(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\right)=C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes F_{\ell+n+\bullet}{\mathscr{D}}_{X}.

The following two theorems describe the generalized eigen-modules in terms of complexes of the induced
{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
-modules.

Theorem 7.1
.

The complex
C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
is a filtered resolution and the characteristic cycle of the
0
-th cohomology is

cc\left({\mathscr{H}}^{0}\left(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\right)\right)=\sum_{J\subset I}|I_{\alpha}\cap J|\cdot\left[T^{*}_{Y^{J}}X\right].

Proof.

Similarly to the proof of Theorem
4.1
and Theorem
4.4
, the associated graded
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}\left(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\right)
can be identified locally with the Koszul complex induced by the regular sequence
(t_{\alpha},D_{1},D_{2},\dots,D_{n})
over
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
, where
t_{\alpha}=\prod_{i\in I_{\alpha}}z_{i}
is the defining equation of
Y_{I_{\alpha}}
. It follows that
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X})
is a resolution and therefore,
C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
is a filtered resolution. We also get that
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{H}}^{0}(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X})
is locally represented by

(7.27)

\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}/(t_{\alpha},D_{1},D_{2},\dots,D_{n}){\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{X},

where

\zeta_{\alpha}=z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}\frac{dz_{1}}{z_{1}}\wedge\frac{dz_{2}}{z_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\frac{dz_{k}}{z_{k}}\wedge dz_{k+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}.

Similar to the calculations in Theorem
4.6
, we get the characteristic cycle is
\sum_{J\subset I}|I_{\alpha}\cap J|\cdot\left[T^{*}_{Y^{J}}X\right]
.
∎

Theorem 7.2
.

There exists a canonical filtered isomorphism

(7.28)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\alpha}:\left({\mathscr{H}}^{0}\left(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\right),F_{\bullet}{\mathscr{H}}^{0}\left(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\right)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha},F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

In particular, the characteristic cycle
cc({\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha})=\sum_{J\subset I}|I_{\alpha}\cap J|\cdot\left[T^{*}_{Y^{J}}X\right]
.

We give the cyclic generators of
{\mathcal{M}}_{\geq\alpha}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{>\alpha}
locally. In principle, this always can be done because every holonomic
{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
-module locally is cyclic.

Lemma 7.3
.

Locally,
{\mathcal{M}}_{\geq\alpha}
is generated by
z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}
, and
{\mathcal{M}}_{>\alpha}
is generated by
z^{\lfloor\alpha\mathbf{e}\rfloor+\mathbf{1}}_{I}
where
\mathbf{1}=(1,1,\dots,1)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{I}
.

Proof.

Let us first check that the class of
z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}
is in
{\mathcal{M}}_{\geq\alpha}
. It suffices to check that it is in

\ker\prod_{i\in I}\prod^{e_{i}-1}_{j=\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil}(R-\frac{j}{e_{i}}).

This can be done by direct calculations:

\displaystyle\prod_{i\in I}\prod^{e_{i}-1}_{j=\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil}(R-\frac{j}{e_{i}})z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in I}\prod^{e_{i}-1}_{j=\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil}(R-\frac{j}{e_{i}})z^{\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil}_{i}=\prod_{i\in I}\prod^{e_{i}-1}_{j=\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil}(\frac{1}{e_{i}}z_{i}\partial_{i}-\frac{j}{e_{i}})z^{\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil}_{i}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in I}\frac{1}{e^{e_{i}-\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil}_{i}}z^{e_{i}}_{i}\partial^{e_{i}-\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil}_{i}=t\prod_{i\in I}\frac{1}{e^{e_{i}-\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil}_{i}}\partial^{e_{i}-\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil}_{i}=0\in{\mathcal{M}}.

Because
R
satisfies the identity (
4.18
),
{\mathcal{M}}_{\geq\alpha}
is also equal to the image of

\prod_{i\in I}\prod^{\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil-1}_{j=0}(R-\frac{j}{e_{i}}).

We conclude from

\prod_{i\in I}\prod^{\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil-1}_{j=0}(R-\frac{j}{e_{i}})(1)=\prod_{i\in I}\prod^{\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil-1}_{j=0}(\frac{1}{e_{i}}z_{i}\partial_{i}-\frac{j}{e_{i}})=z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}\prod_{i\in I}\frac{1}{e^{\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil}_{i}}\partial^{\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil}_{i}

that
z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}\prod_{i\in I}\partial^{\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil}_{i}
generates
{\mathcal{M}}_{\geq\alpha}
. We deduce that
z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}
generates
{\mathcal{M}}_{\geq\alpha}
.
A similar argument works for
{\mathcal{M}}_{>\alpha}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.2
.

As the above lemma,
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
is locally isomorphic to

\zeta\otimes{\left(z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I},D_{1},D_{2},\dots,D_{n}\right){\mathscr{D}}_{X}}/{\left(z^{\lfloor\alpha\mathbf{e}\rfloor+\mathbf{1}}_{I},D_{1},D_{2},\dots,D_{n}\right){\mathscr{D}}_{X}}

where
\zeta=\frac{dz_{1}}{z_{1}}\wedge\frac{dz_{2}}{z_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\frac{dz_{k}}{z_{k}}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}
. Put
\zeta_{\alpha}=z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}\zeta
. Since
{\mathscr{H}}^{0}(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X})
by (
7.26
) is locally isomorphic to

\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}/(t_{\alpha},D_{1}+\alpha_{1},D_{2}+\alpha_{2},\dots,D_{n}+\alpha_{n}){\mathscr{D}}_{X},

the multiplication
{\mathscr{H}}^{0}(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X})\to{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha},\,\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes P\mapsto\zeta\otimes z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}P
is well-defined, does not depend on the coordinate and therefore, gives a filtered morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\alpha}:({\mathscr{H}}^{0}(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}),F_{\bullet}{\mathscr{H}}^{0}(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha},F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}).}

It is clear from the local description that
\phi_{\alpha}
is surjective and hence,

cc\left({\mathscr{H}}^{0}\left(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\right)\right)\geq cc({\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}).

Summing over all the rational numbers
\alpha
in
[0,1)
gives

\sum_{\alpha}cc\left({\mathscr{H}}^{0}\left(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\right)\right)\geq\sum_{\alpha}cc({\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha})=cc({\mathcal{M}}).

On the other hand, by Theorem
4.4
and Theorem
7.1
, the
{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
-module
{\mathcal{M}}
is also successive extensions of
{\mathscr{H}}^{0}\left(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\right)
for
\alpha\in{\mathbb{Q}}\cap[0,1)
. Thus,

\sum_{\alpha}cc\left({\mathscr{H}}^{0}\left(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\right)\right)=cc({\mathcal{M}}).

This forces that
\psi_{\alpha}
must be (filtered) isomorphism.

It remains to show that

(7.29)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\ell}\psi_{\alpha}\colon F_{\ell}{\mathscr{H}}^{0}(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\ell}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha},}

is surjective. Suppose that
z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}P\in{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
represents a class in
F_{\ell}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
. Then we can write

z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}P=P^{\prime}+\sum^{n}_{i=1}D_{i}Q_{i}+z^{\lfloor\alpha\mathbf{e}\rfloor+\mathbf{1}}_{I}T

for
P^{\prime}\in F_{\ell+n}{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
and
T,Q_{i}\in{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
. Regrouping gives

z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}(P-t_{\alpha}T)=P^{\prime}+\sum^{n}_{i=1}D_{i}Q_{i}

By a similar regular sequence argument as in the proof of Theorem
4.4
, we can assume that
P-t_{\alpha}T
is in
F_{\ell+n}{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
. Then the class in
{\mathscr{H}}^{0}(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X})
represented by
P-t_{\alpha}T
is actually in
F_{\ell}{\mathscr{H}}^{0}(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X})
by the local formula. Therefore, we have found a lifting in
F_{\ell}{\mathscr{H}}^{0}(C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X})
(represented by
P
) of the class of
z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}P
in
F_{\ell}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
.
∎

Let
R_{\alpha}\colon=(R-\alpha)
be the nilpotent endomorphism of
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
. One easily gets a nice local formula of
R_{\alpha}
:

Corollary 7.4
.

The endomorphism
R_{\alpha}
of
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
acts locally as
\psi_{\alpha}\circ({\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{d}\,}\otimes\frac{1}{e_{j}}z_{j}\partial_{j})\circ(\psi_{\alpha})^{-1}
for any
j\in I_{\alpha}
. In particular,
R_{\alpha}\colon({\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha},F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha})\to({\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha},F_{\bullet+1}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha})
is a filtered morphism.

Proof.

Because
R-\alpha
acts on the right hand side of the identification (
7.28
) by the left multiplication by
\frac{1}{e_{0}}z_{0}\partial_{0}-\alpha
, a direct calculation

\displaystyle R_{\alpha}\left[\zeta\otimes z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}\right]=

\displaystyle\left[\zeta\otimes\left(\frac{1}{e_{j}}z_{j}\partial_{j}-\alpha\right)\left(z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\zeta\otimes\left(\left(\frac{1}{e_{j}}\lceil\alpha e_{j}\rceil-\alpha\right)z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}+z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}\left(\frac{1}{e_{j}}z_{j}\partial_{j}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{\alpha}{\left[\zeta z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}\otimes\left(\frac{1}{e_{j}}z_{j}\partial_{j}\right)\right]}=\psi_{\alpha}\circ\left({\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{d}\,}\otimes\frac{1}{e_{j}}z_{j}\partial_{j}\right)\circ\psi_{\alpha}^{-1}\left[\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes 1\right]

completes the proof.
∎

7.2.
Strictness of
R_{\alpha}

Similar to the reduced case, every power of
R_{\alpha}
is strict.

Theorem 7.5
.

Any power of
R_{\alpha}
on
({\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha},F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha})
is strict:

(7.30)

R_{\alpha}^{a}F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}=F_{a+\bullet}R_{\alpha}^{a}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha},\quad\text{for any }a\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}.

Let
[R_{\alpha}]
be the endomorphism on
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
induced by
R_{\alpha}
.

Lemma 7.6
.

\ker{[R_{\alpha}]}^{r+1}
is locally generated by monomials of degree
\mu-r
that divide
t_{\alpha}
.

Proof of Theorem
7.5
.

Temporarily admitting this lemma, let
R_{\alpha}^{r+1}m
be an element in
F_{\ell+r+1}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
. Assume that
m\in F_{k}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
. If
k>\ell
then the projection of
R_{\alpha}^{r+1}m
vanishes in
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}_{k+r+1}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
. It follows from the lemma that there are
m^{\prime}\in F_{k-1}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
and
z_{J}=\prod_{j\in J}z_{j}
such that

m=\sum_{{\begin{subarray}{c}|J|=\mu-r,\\
J\subset I_{\alpha}\end{subarray}}}z_{J}m_{J}+m^{\prime}.

Because for every
J\subset I_{\alpha}
of cardinality
r+1
, we can arrange

R^{r+1}_{\alpha}z_{J}=\prod_{j\in I_{\alpha}\setminus J}\frac{1}{e_{j}}z_{j}\partial_{j}z_{J}=t_{\alpha}\prod_{j\in I_{\alpha}}\frac{1}{e_{j}}\partial_{j}=0\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}.

It follows that

(7.31)

\displaystyle R_{\alpha}^{r+1}m=

\displaystyle\sum_{{\begin{subarray}{c}|J|=\mu-r,\\
J\subset I_{\alpha}\end{subarray}}}R_{\alpha}^{r+1}z_{J}m_{J}+R_{\alpha}^{r+1}m^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{{\begin{subarray}{c}|J|=\mu-r,\\
J\subset I_{\alpha}\end{subarray}}}t_{\alpha}m^{\prime}_{J}+R_{\alpha}^{r+1}m^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R_{\alpha}^{r+1}m^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}.

But now
m^{\prime}\in F_{k-1}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
. Iterating (
7.31
), we can find
\tilde{m}\in F_{\ell}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
such that

R_{\alpha}^{r+1}m=R_{\alpha}^{r+1}\tilde{m},

which completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Proof of the lemma.

The proof is essentially the same as the reduced case. We are now working over the commutative ring
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
. We prove by induction on
r
. Without loss of generality, we can assume by abuse of notation that locally
I_{\alpha}=\{0,1,\dots,\mu\}
and
t_{\alpha}=z_{0}z_{1}\cdots z_{\mu}
. Let
P\in{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
represent a class in
\ker[R_{\alpha}]^{r+1}
.

When
r=0
, we have

(7.32)

\frac{1}{e_{0}}z_{0}\partial_{0}P=t_{\alpha}Q_{0}+\sum^{n}_{i=1}D_{i}Q_{i}.

Then
t_{\alpha}Q_{0}
belongs to the ideal generated by

\partial_{0},\partial_{1},\dots,\partial_{\mu},z_{\mu+1}\partial_{\mu+1},z_{\mu+2}\partial_{\mu+2},\dots,z_{k}\partial_{k},\partial_{k+1},\dots\partial_{n}

over
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
. Since
t_{\alpha}
together with the above generators form a regular sequence in
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
, we have
Q_{0}

Q_{0}=\sum^{\mu}_{a=0}\partial_{a}Q_{a}+\sum^{k}_{b=\mu+1}z_{b}\partial_{b}Q_{b}+\sum^{n}_{c=k+1}\partial_{c}Q_{c}.

Substituting this in (
7.32
) and regrouping, we find that

\frac{1}{e_{0}}z_{0}\partial_{0}\left(P-\sum^{\mu}_{a=0}e_{a}\frac{t_{\alpha}}{z_{a}}Q_{a}-\sum^{k}_{b=\mu+1}e_{b}t_{\alpha}Q_{b}\right)\in(D_{1},D_{2},\dots,D_{n}){\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{X}.

Then because
(z_{0}\partial_{0},D_{1},D_{2},\dots,D_{n})
is a regular sequence in
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
,
P
is a linear combination of
{t_{\alpha}}/{z_{a}}
for
a\in\{0,1,\dots,\mu\}
and
D_{1},D_{2},\dots,D_{n}
over
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
. This concludes the case when
r=0
.

Assume the statement is true for the case when the exponent is less than
r
. Because
[R_{\alpha}]
sends the class of
P
to
\ker[R_{\alpha}]^{r}
, by the induction hypothesis we have

(7.33)

\frac{1}{e_{0}}z_{0}\partial_{0}P=\sum_{{\begin{subarray}{c}|J|=\mu-r+1,\\
J\subset I_{\alpha}\end{subarray}}}z_{J}Q_{J}+\sum^{n}_{i=1}D_{i}Q_{i}.

Fixing a subset
J
, we see that
z_{J}Q_{J}
is in the submodule generated by
z_{a},\partial_{b},z_{c}\partial_{c},\partial_{d}
for
a\in I_{\alpha}\setminus J,b\in J,c\in I\setminus I_{\alpha},d\notin I
over
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
. Because those generators together with
z_{J}
form a regular sequence in
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
,
we deduce that

Q_{J}=\sum_{a\in I_{\alpha}\setminus J}z_{a}Q_{a}+\sum_{b\in J}\partial_{b}Q_{b}+\sum_{c\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}z_{c}\partial_{c}Q_{c}+\sum_{d\notin I}\partial_{d}Q_{d}.

Substituting in (
7.33
), we deduce that

\frac{1}{e_{0}}z_{0}\partial_{0}\left(P-\left(\sum_{b\in J}e_{b}\frac{z_{J}}{z_{b}}Q_{b}+\sum_{c\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}e_{c}z_{J}Q_{c}\right)\right)-\sum_{a\in I_{\alpha}\setminus J}z_{J}z_{a}Q_{a}

is in the submodule generated by degree
\mu-r+1
monomials dividing
t_{\alpha}
except
z_{J}
and by
D_{1},D_{2},\dots,D_{n}
over
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
. This means we can reduce
z_{J}Q_{J}
one by one for each
J
on the right-hand side of the equation (
7.33
) and at the last step we find that
\frac{1}{e_{0}}z_{0}\partial_{0}(P-P^{\prime})
is a linear combination of degree
\mu-r+2
monomials dividing
t_{\alpha}
and
D_{1},D_{2},\dots,D_{n}
, where
P^{\prime}
is a linear combination of degree
\mu-r
monomials dividing
t_{\alpha}
.

Note that the left multiplication by
\frac{1}{e_{0}}z_{0}\partial_{0}
has the same effect as applying
[R_{\alpha}]
on
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
. Therefore, the class represented by
P-P^{\prime}

is in
\ker[R_{\alpha}]^{r}
since degree
\mu-r+2
monomials dividing
t_{\alpha}
is in
\ker[R_{\alpha}]^{r-1}
. By the induction hypothesis, the class represented by
P-P^{\prime}
is a linear combination of degree
\mu-r+1
monomials dividing
t_{\alpha}
. Therefore, the class represented by
P
in
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
is a linear combination of degree
\mu-r
monomials dividing
t_{\alpha}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 7.7
.

The
\ker R^{r+1}_{\alpha}
is also generated by degree
\mu-r
monomials dividing
t_{\alpha}
if one identifies
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
locally with
{\mathscr{D}}_{X}/(t_{\alpha},D_{1},D_{2},\dots,D_{n}){\mathscr{D}}_{X}
via (
7.27
) and Theorem
7.2
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as the one of Corollary
5.3
and is left to the readers.
∎

7.3.
The weight filtration

Recall that since
R_{\alpha}
is nilpotent on
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
, it induces a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-indexed filtration
W_{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
.
We filtered the sub-module
W_{r}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
by the induced filtration
F_{\bullet}W_{r}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}=F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}\cap W_{r}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
. Let

{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r}=\frac{\ker R^{r+1}_{\alpha}}{\ker R^{r}_{\alpha}+R_{\alpha}\ker R^{r+2}_{\alpha}}

be the
r
-th primitive part of
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
with the filtration defined by

F_{\ell}{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r}=\text{im}\,\left(F_{\ell}\ker R^{r+1}_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r}\right).

As the formal proof in Theorem
5.6
, we have

Corollary 7.8
.

The morphism
R^{r}_{\alpha}\colon F_{\ell}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{r}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}\to F_{\ell+r}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-r}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
is an isomorphism for
r\geq 0
and the Lefschetz decomposition of
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
respects filtrations, i.e.

F_{\bullet}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{r}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\geq 0,-\frac{r}{2}\end{subarray}}R^{\ell}_{\alpha}F_{\bullet-\ell}{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r+2\ell}\text{ \,for any\, }r\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

7.4.
Summands of the primitive part
{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r}

Recall that
Y^{J}=\bigcap_{j\in J}Y_{j}
and
Y_{J}=\bigcup_{j\in J}Y_{j}
for any subset
J
of
I
and
e_{j}
is the multiplicity of
Y_{j}
in
Y
. Like the reduced case that
{\mathcal{P}}_{r}
decomposes into the direct images of
\omega_{Y^{J}}(-r)
for all index subsets
J
of cardinality
r+1
(Theorem
5.7
), the primitive part
{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r}
of the generalized
\alpha
-eigemodule also decomposes into direct images of certain filtered
{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
-modules
{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}(-r)
for all
J
of cardinality
r+1
such that
e_{j}\alpha
for every
j\in J
is an integer. The filtered
{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
-modules
{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}
comes from cyclic coverings of
Y^{J}
and hence,
{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r}
carries the Hodge theory of the cyclic coverings. In fact, by a well-known construction in
[
EV92
,
\lx@sectionsign
3]
the direct image of the de Rham complex of a cyclic covering decomposes into log de Rham complexes of line bundles. A line bundle with an integrable log connection also can be viewed as a log
{\mathscr{D}}
-module. This suggests that the
{\mathscr{D}}
-modules
{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}
is generated by a certain log
{\mathscr{D}}
-module
{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J}
. If
Y
is reduced and
\alpha=0
,
{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}
is just
\omega_{Y^{J}}
. We shall construct auxiliary log
{\mathscr{D}}
-modules
{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J}
whose log de Rham complex will be used to construct the
{\mathscr{D}}
-module
{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}
, without using cyclic cover. The cyclic coverings are involved only when we study the Hodge theory of those
{\mathscr{D}}
-modules. We fix a rational number
\alpha\in[0,1)
and let
I_{\alpha}
be a subset of indices consisting of
i
such that
\alpha e_{i}
is an integer.

Denote by
{\mathcal{L}}
the line bundle
{\mathscr{O}}_{X}\left(-\sum_{i\in I_{\alpha}}{\frac{e_{i}}{N}}Y_{i}\right)
, where
N
is the greatest common divisor of
e_{i}
for
i\in I_{\alpha}
. Then we have
{\mathscr{O}}_{X}\left(-\left\lceil\alpha Y\right\rceil\right)={\mathcal{L}}^{\alpha N}\left(-\sum_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}\left\lceil\alpha e_{i}Y_{i}\right\rceil\right)
. As the line bundle
{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(Y)
can be trivialized by a global section, there is an isomorphism of
{\mathscr{O}}_{X}
-modules:

(7.34)

{\mathcal{L}}^{N}={\mathscr{O}}_{X}\left(-\sum_{i\in I_{\alpha}}e_{i}Y_{i}\right)\to{\mathscr{O}}_{X}\left(\sum_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}e_{i}Y_{i}\right).

Choose a local section
l
of
{\mathcal{L}}
such that
l^{N}\mapsto\prod_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}z^{-e_{i}}_{i}
under (
7.34
). We shall define a log connection
\nabla
on

{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)={\mathcal{L}}^{\alpha{N}}\left(-\sum_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}\left\lceil\alpha e_{i}Y_{i}\right\rceil\right).

First, we define, using the product rule

(7.35)

\frac{\nabla l^{N}}{l^{N}}=N\frac{\nabla l}{l}=\sum_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}-e_{i}\frac{dz_{i}}{z_{i}}

due to (
7.34
). Then, let
s=l^{\alpha N}\prod_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}z^{\left\lceil\alpha e_{i}\right\rceil}_{i}
be the local frame of
{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)
. Noting that
\alpha N
is a non-negative integer, the induced log connection works as

(7.36)

\displaystyle\frac{\nabla s}{s}=\frac{\nabla(l^{\alpha N}\prod_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}z^{\left\lceil\alpha e_{i}\right\rceil}_{i})}{l^{\alpha N}\prod_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}z^{\left\lceil\alpha e_{i}\right\rceil}_{i}}

\displaystyle=\alpha N\frac{\nabla l}{l}+\sum_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}{\left\lceil\alpha e_{i}\right\rceil}\frac{dz_{i}}{z_{i}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}\left({\left\lceil\alpha e_{i}\right\rceil}-\alpha e_{i}\right)\frac{dz_{i}}{z_{i}}=\sum_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}\{-\alpha e_{i}\}\frac{dz_{i}}{z_{i}},

where
\{-\}
denotes the function of taking fractional part. Putting in more standard form,

\nabla s=\sum_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}\{-\alpha e_{i}\}\frac{dz_{i}}{z_{i}}\otimes s.

This log connection is integrable and has poles along
Y_{i}
for
i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}
with eigenvalues
\{-\alpha e_{i}\}
. We endow the line bundle
{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)
with this integrable log connection
\nabla
.

Fix a subset
J
of
I_{\alpha}
with
|J|=r+1
so that
\dim Y^{J}=n-r
. The pullback of
({\mathscr{O}}_{X}(\lceil-\alpha Y\rceil),\nabla)
by the inclusion
\tau^{J}\colon Y^{J}\to X
gives an integrable log connection
(\mathscr{V},\nabla)=({\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J},\nabla)
on
Y^{J}
with poles along
E=E^{\alpha,J}
the pullback of
Y_{I\setminus I_{\alpha}}
. Moreover, the log de Rham complex of
({\mathscr{V}},\nabla)

\{{\mathscr{V}}\to\Omega_{Y^{J}}(\log E)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}\to\cdots\to\Omega^{{n-r}}_{Y^{J}}(\log E)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}\}[{n-r}],

induces a complex of
{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
-modules (indeed, the log de Rham complex of
{\mathscr{V}}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
):

(7.37)

\{{\mathscr{V}}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}\to\Omega_{Y^{J}}(\log E)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}\to\cdots\to\Omega^{{n-r}}_{Y^{J}}(\log E)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}\}[{n-r}],

with the filtration by the subcomplexes for
\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}
:

\{{\mathscr{V}}\otimes F_{\ell}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}\to\Omega_{Y^{J}}(\log E)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}\otimes F_{\ell+1}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}\to\cdots\to\Omega^{{n-r}}_{Y^{J}}(\log E)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}\otimes F_{\ell+{n-r}}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}\}[{n-r}].

We will prove that the complex (
7.37
) is a filtered resolution a filtered
{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
-module
({\mathcal{V}},F)=({\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J},F)
in Lemma
7.9
. For example, if
\alpha=0
, then
E
is empty and
{\mathscr{V}}
is just
{\mathscr{O}}_{Y^{J}}
so that
{\mathcal{V}}=\omega_{Y^{J}}
as
{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
-modules. Since the eigenvalues of the log connection are in
(0,1)
if poles exist, the log de Rham complex of
({\mathscr{V}},\nabla)
is the minimal extension
R_{!*}\mathbb{V}
of the local system
\mathbb{V}
consisting of the flat sections of
\nabla
on
{\mathscr{V}}
over
Y^{J}\setminus Y_{I\setminus J}
(see
[
EV92
, 1.6]
). Later we will put a sesquilinear pairing on
{\mathcal{V}}
and all the data will yield a pure Hodge structure of the log de Rham complex of
{\mathscr{V}}
.

Lemma 7.9
.

The de Rham complex
{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Y^{J}}{\mathcal{V}}
together with the filtration
F_{\bullet}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Y^{J}}{\mathcal{V}}
is isomorphic to the log de Rham complex
\Omega^{n-r+\bullet}_{Y^{J}}(\log E)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}
with the stupid filtration in the derived category of filtered complexes of
{\mathbb{C}}
-vector spaces. In addition,
{\mathcal{V}}
is holonomic and the characteristic cycle of
{\mathcal{V}}
is

cc({\mathcal{V}})=\sum_{K\subset I\setminus I_{\alpha}}\left[T^{*}_{Y^{K\cup{J}}}Y^{J}\right].

Proof.

We can choose the local frame
s
of
{\mathscr{V}}
such that

\nabla s=\sum_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}\frac{dz_{i}}{z_{i}}\otimes\{-\alpha e_{i}\}s

where
z_{i}
is the defining equation of
E_{i}
for each
i
. Therefore, the complex (
7.37
) locally is the Koszul complex over
{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
associated to the sequence

x_{1}\partial_{1}+\{-\alpha e_{1}\},x_{2}\partial_{2}+\{-\alpha e_{2}\},\dots,x_{p}\partial_{p}+\{-\alpha e_{p}\},\partial_{p+1},\partial_{p+2},\dots,\partial_{n-r},

for some rearrangement of coordinates and under the trivialization of
{\mathscr{V}}
given by
s
. It follows that the associated graded of (
7.37
) is the Koszul complex associated to the regular sequence

x_{1}\partial_{1},x_{2}\partial_{2},\dots,x_{p}\partial_{\nu},\partial_{p+1},\partial_{p+2},\dots,\partial_{n-r}

over
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
. Thus, the complex (
7.37
) is a filtered resolution. By a similar argument in Theorem
4.4
, the
{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
-module
{\mathcal{V}}
is holonomic and the characteristic cycle
cc({\mathcal{V}})=\sum_{K\subset I\setminus I_{\alpha}}\left[T^{*}_{Y^{K\cup{J}}}Y^{J}\right]
.

Moreover, we have isomorphisms in the derived category of complexes of
{\mathbb{C}}
-vector spaces:

\displaystyle F_{\ell}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Y^{J}}{\mathcal{V}}=F_{\ell+*}{\mathcal{V}}\otimes\bigwedge^{-\star}{\mathscr{T}}_{Y^{J}}

\displaystyle\simeq\Omega^{n-r+\bullet}_{Y^{J}}(\log E)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}\otimes F_{\ell+n-r+\bullet+*}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}\otimes\bigwedge^{-\star}{\mathscr{T}}_{Y^{J}}

\displaystyle\simeq\Omega^{n-r+\bullet}_{Y^{J}}(\log E)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}\otimes F_{\ell+n-r+\bullet}{\mathscr{O}}_{Y^{J}}.

The complex
\Omega^{n-r+\bullet}_{Y^{J}}(\log E)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}\otimes F_{\ell+n-r}{\mathscr{O}}_{Y^{J}}
is the stupid filtration on the log de Rham complex on
{\mathscr{V}}
, since
F_{\ell}{\mathscr{O}}_{Y^{J}}
is
{\mathscr{O}}_{Y^{J}}
or vanishes if
\ell<0
.
∎

We also need an auxiliary
{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
-module
{\mathcal{V}}^{*}_{\alpha,J}
to identify the primitive part
{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r}
which plays the role as
\omega_{Y^{J}}(*D^{J})
in the counterpart for the reduced case (Theorem
5.7
). The log de Rham complex of
({\mathscr{V}},\nabla)
can be enlarged into

\{{\mathscr{V}}\to\Omega_{Y^{J}}(\log D)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}\to\cdots\to\Omega^{{n-r}}_{Y^{J}}(\log D)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}\}[{n-r}],

for
D=D^{J}
the pullback of the divisor
Y_{I\setminus J}
. It is quasi-isomorphic to
Rj_{*}\mathbb{V}
for
j\colon Y^{J}-Y_{I\setminus J}\to Y^{J}
is the open immersion. By a similar argument of the above, it induces a complex of filtered
{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
-modules

(7.38)

\{{\mathscr{V}}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}\to\Omega_{Y^{J}}(\log D)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}\to\cdots\to\Omega^{{n-r}}_{Y^{J}}(\log D)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}\}[{n-r}]

which is a filtered resolution
{\mathcal{V}}^{*}={\mathcal{V}}^{*}_{\alpha,J}
with the filtration
F_{\ell}{\mathcal{V}}^{*}=F_{\ell}{\mathcal{V}}^{*}_{\alpha,J}
induced by the subcomplex

\{{\mathscr{V}}\otimes F_{\ell}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}\to\Omega_{Y^{J}}(\log D)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}\otimes F_{\ell+1}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}\to\cdots\to\Omega^{{n-r}}_{Y^{J}}(\log D)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}\otimes F_{\ell+{n-r}}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}\}[{n-r}].

We naturally get an induced morphism
({\mathcal{V}},F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{V}})\to({\mathcal{V}}^{*},F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{V}}^{*})
from the inclusion of the log de Rham complexes.

Lemma 7.10
.

The canonical morphism
({\mathcal{V}},F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{V}})\to({\mathcal{V}}^{*},F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{V}}^{*})
is strictly injective, and its image is generated by the monomials defining
D-E
.

Proof.

Suppose
x_{1}x_{2}\cdots x_{p}
is the local defining equation of
E
and
x_{1}x_{2}\cdots x_{q}
is the local defining equation of
D
for
q\geq p+1
. Since
{\mathcal{V}}
is locally generated by the class of

\bigwedge^{p}_{i=1}\frac{dx_{i}}{x_{i}}\wedge dx_{p+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dx_{n-r}\otimes s\otimes 1

and
{\mathcal{V}}^{*}
is locally generated by the class of

\bigwedge^{q}_{i=1}\frac{dx_{i}}{x_{i}}\wedge dx_{q+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dx_{n-r}\otimes s\otimes 1,

the image is generated by the class of
\bigwedge^{q}_{i=1}\frac{dx_{i}}{x_{i}}\wedge dx_{q+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dx_{n-r}\otimes s\otimes x_{p+1}x_{p+2}\cdots x_{q}
. The morphism locally can be viewed as

\displaystyle{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}/(x_{1}\partial_{1}+r_{1},\dots,x_{p}\partial_{p}+r_{p},\partial_{p+1},\dots,\partial_{n-r}){\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}\to

\displaystyle{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}/(x_{1}\partial_{1}+r_{1},\dots,x_{q}\partial_{q}+r_{q},\partial_{q+1},\dots,\partial_{n-r}){\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}},

with
[P]\mapsto[x_{p+1}x_{p+2}\cdots x_{q}P]
where
r_{1},r_{2},\dots,r_{p}
are the eigenvalues of
\nabla
on
{\mathscr{V}}
and
r_{p+1}=r_{p+2}=\cdots=r_{q}=0
. Since

\Omega^{n-r}_{Y^{J}}(\log E)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}=F_{-(n-r)}{\mathcal{V}}\to F_{-(n-r)}{\mathcal{V}}^{*}=\Omega^{n-r}_{Y^{J}}(\log D)\otimes{\mathscr{V}}

is injective, it suffices to show that
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathcal{V}}^{*}
is injective by induction. Due to the complexes (
7.37
) and (
7.38
) are filtered resolutions, the morphism on the associated graded modules corresponds to the map,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}/(x_{1}\partial_{1},\dots,x_{p}\partial_{p},\partial_{p+1},\dots,\partial_{n-r}){\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}\to

\displaystyle{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}/(x_{1}\partial_{1},\dots,x_{q}\partial_{q},\partial_{q+1},\dots,\partial_{n-r}){\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}},

with
[P]\mapsto[x_{p+1}x_{p+2}\cdots x_{q}P]
. By another induction on the number of components of
D-E
, we can assume
q=p+1
. Let
P\in{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
represent a class in the kernel of the above morphism. Then we have

x_{q}P=\sum^{q}_{i=1}x_{i}\partial_{i}P_{i}+\sum^{n-r}_{j=q+1}\partial_{j}P_{j}\in{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}.

Subtracting
x_{q}\partial_{q}P_{q}
on both sides gives

x_{q}(P-\partial_{q}P_{q})=\sum^{q-1}_{i=1}x_{i}\partial_{i}P_{i}+\sum^{n-r}_{j=q+1}\partial_{j}P_{j}\in{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}.

Since
x_{q},x_{1}\partial_{1},\dots,x_{q-1}\partial_{q-1},\partial_{q+1},\dots,\partial_{n-r}
is a regular sequence over
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
,

(P-\partial_{q}P_{q})=\sum^{q-1}_{i=1}x_{i}\partial_{i}P^{\prime}_{i}+\sum^{n-r}_{j=q+1}\partial_{j}P^{\prime}_{j}\in{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}.

Hence,
P
is a linear combination of
x_{1}\partial_{1},x_{2}\partial_{2},\dots,x_{p}\partial_{p},\partial_{p+1},\dots,\partial_{n-r}
over
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{F}{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
, which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 7.11
.

One can utilize the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence to establish that
{\mathcal{V}}
corresponds to the minimal extension of
{\mathscr{V}}|_{Y^{J}\setminus D}
, while
{\mathcal{V}}^{*}
corresponds to the
*
-extension of
{\mathscr{V}}|_{Y^{J}\setminus D}
. As a result, we observe that
{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{V}}^{*}
is an injective map. However, in our situation, this argument is overkill and does not capture the strictness, namely
F_{\ell}{\mathcal{V}}=F_{\ell}{\mathcal{V}}^{*}\cap{\mathcal{V}}
.

Putting in more general notations and summarizing what we have proved in the above two lemmas:

Theorem 7.12
.

The filtered
{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
-module
({\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J},F_{\bullet})
is holonomic and its de Rham complex
{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Y^{J}}{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}
together with the induced filtration is isomorphic to the log de Rham complex
\Omega^{n-r+\bullet}_{Y^{J}}(\log E^{\alpha,J})\otimes{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J}
with the stupid filtration in the derived category of filtered complexes of
{\mathbb{C}}
-vector spaces and whose characteristic cycle is

cc({\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J})=\sum_{K\subset I\setminus I_{\alpha}}\left[T^{*}_{Y^{K\cup J}}Y^{J}\right].

The canonical filtered morphism
({\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J},F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J})\to({\mathcal{V}}^{*}_{\alpha,J},F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{V}}^{*}_{\alpha,J})
is filtered injective and the image is generated by the monomial defining the divisor
D^{J}-E^{\alpha,J}
.

7.5.
Identifying the primitive part
{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r}

We are going to identify the
r
-th primitive part
({\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r},F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r})
with a direct sum of
{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}(-r)
for
J
ranging over subsets
I_{\alpha}
of cardinality
r+1
. The argument is parallel to the one of the reduced case (Theorem
5.7
).

Theorem 7.13
.

Let
{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,r}=\bigoplus_{J}\tau^{J}_{+}{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}
for
J
running over the subsets of
I_{\alpha}
of cardinality
r+1
, where
\tau^{J}:Y^{J}\hookrightarrow X
is the closed embedding. Then there exists an isomorphism
\phi_{\alpha,r}:({\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r},F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r})\to{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,r}(-r)

between filtered
{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
-modules.

Proof.

Because the log connection (
7.34
) we constructed on
{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)
has zero residue on
Y_{i}
for
i\in I_{\alpha}
, we have the residue morphism between log de Rham complexes.

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{Y^{J}}:\Omega^{\bullet+n+1}_{X}(\log Y)\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)|_{Y_{I_{\alpha}}}

\displaystyle\to\Omega^{\bullet+n-r}_{Y^{J}}(\log D^{J})\otimes{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J},

where
D^{J}
is the pullback of
Y_{I\setminus J}
and
J\subset I_{\alpha}
of cardinality
r+1
,
up to a sign depending on the order of the indices. Denote by
B^{\bullet}_{\alpha}
the log de Rham complex
\Omega^{\bullet+n+1}_{X}(\log Y)\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)
of
{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)
. The residue morphism
{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{Y^{J}}
extends to a morphism of the complexes of induced
{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
-modules

{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{Y^{J}}:B^{\bullet}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{I_{\alpha}}}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\to\Omega^{\bullet+n-r}_{Y^{J}}(\log D^{J})\otimes{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}.

Let
{\mathcal{H}}^{\ell}_{\alpha}\colon={\mathcal{H}}^{\ell}\left(B^{\bullet}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{I_{\alpha}}}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\right)
. Then we obtain a filtered morphism
{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{Y^{J}}:{\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{V}}^{*}_{\alpha,J}
by taking cohomology. Let
{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{\alpha,r}=\bigoplus{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{Y^{J}}:{\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{V}}^{*}_{\alpha,r}(-r)
where
{\mathcal{V}}^{*}_{\alpha,r}=\bigoplus_{J}{\mathcal{V}}^{*}_{\alpha,J}
for
J
running over cardinality
r+1
subsets of
I_{\alpha}
. Because
\frac{dt}{t}\wedge:\Omega^{\bullet+n}_{X/\Delta}(\log Y)(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)\to\Omega^{\bullet+n+1}_{X}(\log Y)(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)
also extends to the complexes of the induced
{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
-modules, we get a short exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\bullet-1}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\bullet-1}|_{Y_{I_{\alpha}}}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{dt}{t}\wedge}

The associated long exact sequence gives

(7.39)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{dt}{t}\wedge}

By pre-composing
\frac{dt}{t}\wedge
, we get a morphism

{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{\alpha,J}\circ\frac{dt}{t}\wedge:{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{V}}^{*}_{\alpha,r}(-r),\quad[\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes P]\to[{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{\alpha,J}\frac{dt}{t}\wedge\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes P].

Recall that every element in
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
is locally represented by
\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes P
for
\zeta_{\alpha}=z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}\frac{dz_{1}}{z_{1}}\wedge\frac{dz_{2}}{z_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\frac{dz_{k}}{z_{k}}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}
given that locally
I=\{0,1,\dots,k\}
, and
P\in{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
. By Corollary
7.7
, every class in
\ker R^{r+1}_{\alpha}
is represented by a linear combination of
\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes z_{\overline{J}}P
for some ordered index subset
J
of
I_{\alpha}
of cardinality
r+1
and
\overline{J}
is the complement of
J
in
I_{\alpha}
and
z_{\overline{J}}=\prod_{j\in\overline{J}}z_{j}
. Thus, its image under the above morphism is only contained in the component
{\mathcal{V}}^{*}_{\alpha,J}(-r)
because
z_{\overline{J}}
vanishes on other components. The image is the class represented by

(7.40)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{\alpha,J}\frac{dz_{0}}{z_{0}}\wedge\frac{dz_{1}}{z_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\frac{dz_{k}}{z_{k}}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}\otimes z_{\overline{J}}P

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{dz_{I\setminus J}}{z_{I\setminus J}}\wedge dz_{k+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}\otimes s_{\alpha,J}\otimes z_{\overline{J}}P\in\Omega^{n-r}_{Y^{J}}\otimes{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X},

where
s_{\alpha,J}
is the local frame of
{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J}
by restricting
z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}
and the sign is depending on the order of
J
. It also follows from the calculation that the image does not have a pole along the pull-back of
Y_{\overline{J}}
. Hence, it is contained in the subsheaf consisting of classes represented by
\Omega^{n-r}_{Y^{J}}(\log E^{\alpha,J})\otimes{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
, where
E^{\alpha,J}
is the pull-back of
Y_{I\setminus I_{\alpha}}
so that
D^{J}-E^{\alpha,J}
is the pull-back of
Y_{\overline{J}}
. This means that the image of the class represented by (
7.40
) is also in the image of the canonical inclusion:

\displaystyle\tau^{J}_{+}{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}(-r)\hookrightarrow\tau^{J}_{+}{\mathcal{V}}^{*}_{\alpha,J}

\displaystyle(-r),

\displaystyle[dz_{\overline{J}}\wedge\frac{dz_{I\setminus I_{\alpha}}}{z_{I\setminus I_{\alpha}}}\wedge dz_{k+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}\otimes s_{\alpha,J}\otimes P]

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle[\frac{dz_{\overline{J}}}{z_{\overline{J}}}\wedge\frac{dz_{I\setminus I_{\alpha}}}{z_{I\setminus I_{\alpha}}}\wedge dz_{k+1}\wedge

\displaystyle\cdots\wedge dz_{n}\otimes s_{\alpha,J}\otimes z_{\overline{J}}P].

See Theorem
7.12
. Therefore, there is a filtered morphism
\rho_{\alpha,r}
making the following diagram commute.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ker R_{\alpha}^{r+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,r}(-r)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{V}}^{*}_{\alpha,r}(-r)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\alpha,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\frac{dt}{t}\wedge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{\alpha,r}}

The kernel of
\rho_{\alpha,r}
contains
\ker R_{\alpha}^{r}
: for an element in
\ker R_{\alpha}^{r}
locally represented by
\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes z_{K}P
for
K
a subset of
I_{\alpha}
such that the cardinality of
I_{\alpha}\setminus K
is
r
, its image under
\rho_{\alpha,r}
is zero because
z_{K}
annihilates all
\Omega^{n-r}_{Y^{J}}(\log D^{J})\otimes{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J}
for any
J\subset I_{\alpha}
of cardinality
r+1
. The morphism
\rho_{\alpha,r}
also kills
R_{\alpha}\ker R^{r+2}_{\alpha}
because
\frac{dt}{t}\wedge
vanishes on the image of
R_{\alpha}
by (
7.39
). It follows that
\rho_{\alpha,r}
factors through a filtered morphism

\phi_{\alpha,r}:{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r}=\frac{\ker R^{r+1}_{\alpha}}{\ker R^{r}_{\alpha}+R_{\alpha}\ker R^{r+2}_{\alpha}}\to{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,r}(-r).

For
dz_{\overline{J}}\wedge\frac{dz_{I\setminus I_{\alpha}}}{z_{I\setminus I_{\alpha}}}\wedge dz_{k+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}\otimes s_{\alpha,J}\otimes P\in\Omega^{n-r}_{Y^{J}}(\log E^{\alpha,J})\otimes{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J}\otimes F_{\ell}{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
representing a class in
F_{\ell}\tau^{J}_{+}{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}(-r)
where
J\subset I_{\alpha}
of cardinality
r+1
, we can find a lifting represented by
\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes z_{\overline{J}}P
in
F_{\ell}\ker R^{r+1}_{\alpha}
, which means

F_{\ell}\ker R_{\alpha}^{r+1}\to F_{\ell+r}{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,r}

is surjective, i.e. the morphism
\phi_{\alpha,r}
is filtered surjective. We prove that
\phi_{\alpha,r}
is a (filtered) isomorphism by counting the characteristic cycles as in Theorem
5.7
. Because
\phi_{\alpha,r}
is surjective, one gets

cc({\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r})\geq cc({\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,r}).

It follows from Corollary
7.12
that

cc({\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,r})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset I_{\alpha},\\
|J|=r+1\end{subarray}}cc(\tau^{J}_{+}{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset I_{\alpha},\\
|J|=r+1\end{subarray}}\sum_{K\subset I\setminus I_{\alpha}}\left[T^{*}_{Y^{J\cup K}}X\right]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset I,\\
|J\cap I_{\alpha}|=r+1\end{subarray}}\left[T^{*}_{Y^{J}}X\right].

On the other hand, by the Lefschetz decomposition and Theorem
7.2
,

\displaystyle\sum_{J\subset I}|J\cap I_{\alpha}|\cdot\left[T^{*}_{Y^{J}}X\right]

\displaystyle=cc({\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha})=cc({\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha})=\sum_{r\geq 0}(r+1)cc({\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{r\geq 0}(r+1)cc({\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,r})

\displaystyle=\sum_{r\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset I,\\
|J\cap I_{\alpha}|=r+1\end{subarray}}(r+1)\left[T^{*}_{Y^{J}}X\right]=\sum_{J\subset I}|J\cap I_{\alpha}|\cdot\left[T^{*}_{Y^{J}}X\right].

It follows that all inequalities must be equalities and in particular,

cc({\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r})=cc({\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,r})

from which we conclude that
\phi_{\alpha,r}
is an isomorphism between (filtered)
{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
-modules.
∎

8.
Non-reduced case: Sesquilinear pairing and limiting mixed Hodge structure

8.1.
Kähler package of cyclic covering

To accomplish our goal, we need to show that the graded vector space

\bigoplus_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(Y^{J},\Omega^{\bullet+n-r}_{Y^{J}}(\log E^{\alpha,J})\otimes{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J}\right)

underlies a polarized Hodge-Lefschetz structure and satisfies the Hard Lefschetz property. This will allow us to establish the hypercohomology of the de Rham complex of the primitive part
{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r}
inherits these properties by Theorem
7.12
and Theorem
7.13
. To achieve this, we will utilize the geometry of cyclic coverings.

We first give another description of the integrable log connection (
7.34
) using cyclic coverings. Fix a rational number
\alpha
in
[0,1)
, Because the isomorphism,

{\mathcal{L}}^{N}={\mathscr{O}}_{X}\left(-\sum_{i\in I_{\alpha}}e_{i}Y_{i}\right)\to{\mathscr{O}}_{X}\left(\sum_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}e_{i}Y_{i}\right),

we obtain a cyclic covering
\pi_{\alpha}:X_{\alpha}\to X
by taking the
N
-th roots out of
\sum_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}e_{i}Y_{i}
and normalizing it. The direct image
{\pi_{\alpha}}_{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{X_{\alpha}}
decomposes into eigenspaces with respect the Galois action as well as the direct image of exterior differential
{\pi_{\alpha}}_{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{X_{\alpha}}\to{\pi_{\alpha}}_{*}\Omega_{X_{\alpha}}

[
EV92
, Theorem 3.2]
. The line bundle

{\mathcal{L}}^{\alpha{N}}\left(-\sum_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}\left\lceil\alpha e_{i}Y_{i}\right\rceil\right),

is the
\alpha
-eigenspaces of
{\pi_{\alpha}}_{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{X_{\alpha}}
for some suitable choice of a generator of the Galois group. Because the decomposition respects the exterior differential, we obtained an integrable log connection with eigenvalues
\{\alpha e_{i}\}
along
Y_{i}
for each
i\in I_{\alpha}
. Note that
X_{\alpha}
might be singular.

Let
J\subset I_{\alpha}
be a subset of cardinality
r+1
. Since
Y^{J}
is not contained in
Y_{I\setminus I_{\alpha}}
, the fiber product
Y^{J}_{\alpha}=X_{\alpha}\times_{X}Y^{J}
is again a cyclic covering of
Y^{J}
by taking the
N
-th roots out of
\sum_{i\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}e_{i}Y_{i}\cap Y^{J}
. Let
\pi^{J}_{\alpha}:Y^{J}_{\alpha}\to Y^{J}
be the second projection.

(8.41)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{J}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{J}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{J}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{J}}

We conclude that
({\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J},\nabla)
is the
\alpha
-eigenspace of
{\pi^{J}_{\alpha}}_{*}({\mathscr{O}}_{Y^{J}_{\alpha}},d)
. The log de Rham complex of
({\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J},\nabla)
is a summand of the direct image of the de Rham complex
{\pi^{J}_{\alpha}}_{*}\Omega^{\bullet+n-r}_{Y^{J}_{\alpha}}
of
Y^{J}_{\alpha}
.

We shall work in the general setting and adopt the convention in
[
EV86
]
and
[
EV92
]
. Let
{\mathcal{L}}
be a line bundle on a Kähler manifold
Z
with a Kähler form
\omega
and
D=\sum_{i}\nu_{i}D_{i}
be a simple normal crossings divisor such that for some
N>1
one has
{\mathcal{L}}^{N}={\mathscr{O}}_{Z}(D)
. Define
{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}={\mathcal{L}}^{j}(-\lfloor\frac{jD}{N}\rfloor)
for
1\leq j\leq N-1
. One puts an integrable logarithmic connection on
{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}
with poles along
D^{(j)}
, where

D^{(j)}=\sum_{\frac{j\nu_{i}}{N}\notin{\mathbb{Z}}}D_{i}.

Let
\iota:U\hookrightarrow Z
be the complement of
D
and
\mathbb{V}
is the underlying local system of
{\mathcal{L}}|_{U}
. Let
\tau:Z^{\prime}\to Z
be the cyclic covering obtained by first taking
N
-th root out of
D
then taking the normalization and
\pi:\tilde{Z}\to Z^{\prime}
be a log resolution of singularity equivariant with respect to the Galois group
{\mathrm{G}}{\mathrm{a}}{\mathrm{l}\,}(Z^{\prime}/Z)=\langle\sigma\rangle
and let
E
be the simple normal crossing exceptional divisor.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}

Note that
\tilde{Z}
is Kähler because it is a resolution of a subvariety of the geometric line bundle of
{\mathcal{L}}
, which is Kähler, although the induced Kähler class does not relate well with
\omega
on
X
. The pull-back
\eta^{*}\omega
is only positive over
\tilde{U}=\eta^{-1}(U)
, but one can still cook up a Kähler class by adding a small multiple of the first Chern class
\Theta\in H^{2}(\tilde{Z},{\mathbb{Z}}(1))
of the relative ample line bundle of the projective morphism
\pi:\tilde{Z}\to Z^{\prime}
. We can assume
\Theta
is invariant under
\sigma
by averaging it.

Lemma 8.1
.

In the given notation, the cohomology class
[\eta^{*}\omega]+\lambda(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{-1}\Theta\in H^{1,1}(Z)\cap H^{2}(Z,{\mathbb{R}})
is an invariant Kähler class if
\lambda
is a sufficient small positive number.

Proof.

Let
\tilde{D}_{i}
be the strict transformation of
\tau^{-1}(D_{i})
and
s_{i}\in H^{0}(\tilde{Z},{\mathscr{O}}_{\tilde{Z}}(\tilde{D}_{i}))
whose zero locus is
\tilde{D}_{i}
. Let
h_{i}
be a Hermitian metric on each line bundle
{\mathscr{O}}_{\tilde{Z}}(\tilde{D}_{i})
and
\rho_{i}
be sufficient small positive bump function supported in a small neighborhood of
\tilde{D}_{i}
for each
i
. Then the
(1,1)
-form

\eta^{*}\omega+\sum_{i}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\partial\bar{\partial}\rho_{i}h_{i}(s_{i},s_{i})

is positive on
\tilde{Z}-E
but only semi-positive over
E
. However, the class
(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{-1}\Theta
is positive over
E
. Therefore, for
\lambda
sufficient small positive, the class of

\eta^{*}\omega+\sum_{i}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\partial\bar{\partial}\rho_{i}h_{i}(s_{i},s_{i})+\lambda(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{-1}\Theta

is a Kähler class. But
\partial\bar{\partial}\rho_{i}h_{i}(s_{i},s_{i})
is exact. The cohomology class of above just equals
[\eta^{*}\omega]+\lambda(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{-1}\Theta\in H^{1,1}(\tilde{Z})\cap H^{2}(Z,{\mathbb{R}})
. It is invariant because both
[\eta^{*}\omega]
and
\Theta
are invariant.
∎

Lemma 8.2
.

The hypercohomology
{\mathbf{H}}^{k}\left(Z,\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\right)
is a summand of
\xi^{-j}
-eigenspace of
H^{k}(\tilde{Z})
, and thus it is a sub-Hodge structure of weight
k
.

Proof.

By
(1.6)
in
[
EV86
]
that
R\iota_{!}\mathbb{V}^{-j}
,
R\iota_{*}\mathbb{V}^{-j}
and
\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}
are all quasi-isomorphic, which implies that

{\mathbf{H}}^{k}\left(Z,\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\right)\simeq H^{k}_{c}(U,\mathbb{V}^{-j})\simeq H^{k}(U,\mathbb{V}^{-j}).

Because
\eta
is étale over
U
,
H^{k}(U,\mathbb{V}^{j})
(resp.
H^{k}_{c}(U,\mathbb{V}^{j})
) is a
\xi^{j}
-eigenspace of
H^{k}(\tilde{U},{\mathbb{C}})
(resp.
H^{k}_{c}(\tilde{U},{\mathbb{C}})
) for the cyclic action
\sigma
, where
\xi
is a
N
-th root of unity. Then the canonical morphisms of mixed Hodge structures

(8.42)

H^{k}_{c}(\tilde{U})\to H^{k}(\tilde{Z})\to H^{k}(\tilde{U})

respect the eigenspaces decomposition because we make
\tilde{Z}
equivariant.
∎

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
{\mathsf{X}}={2\pi\sqrt{-1}}L
where
L=[\omega]\wedge
is the Lefschetz operator on
Z
. For
0\leq k\leq n
, the following two statements hold:

(1)

Hard Lefschetz is valid on the hypercohomology, i.e.

{\mathsf{X}}^{k}:{\mathbf{H}}^{n-k}\left(Z,\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\right)\to{\mathbf{H}}^{n+k}\left(Z,\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\right)(k)

is an isomorphism of Hodge structures.

(2)

The pairing

(8.43)

(m^{\prime},\overline{m^{\prime\prime}})\mapsto\frac{\varepsilon(n-k+1)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{n}}\int_{\tilde{Z}}\eta^{*}\left({\mathsf{X}}^{k}m^{\prime}\wedge\overline{m^{\prime\prime}}\right)

is a polarization on the primitive part of
{\mathbf{H}}^{n-k}\left(Z,\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\right)
, where
\eta^{*}\left({\mathsf{X}}^{k}\alpha\wedge\overline{\beta}\right)
is the top form determined by the inclusion

\eta^{*}\Omega^{n}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\subset\omega_{\tilde{Z}}.

Proof.

Let
\tilde{L}=[\eta^{*}\omega+\lambda\Theta]\wedge
be the Lefschetz operator on
\tilde{Z}
. Then the Hard Lefschetz on
\tilde{Z}
says

\tilde{\mathsf{X}}^{k}:H^{n-k}(\tilde{Z})\to H^{n+k}(\tilde{Z})(k)

is an isomorphism, where
\tilde{\mathsf{X}}\colon=2\pi\sqrt{-1}\tilde{L}
. Because
\tilde{L}
is invariant and respects the morphisms in (
8.42
), the above isomorphism is compatible with eigenspaces decomposition, it follows that

(8.44)

{\tilde{\mathsf{X}}}^{k}:{\mathbf{H}}^{n-k}\left(Z,\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\right)\to{\mathbf{H}}^{n+k}\left(Z,\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\right)\left(k\right)

is injective by Lemma
8.2
. In fact, the
\xi^{i}
-eigenspace of
H^{k}_{c}(\tilde{U})
is orthogonal to the
\xi^{j}
-eigenspace of
H^{2n-k}(\tilde{U})
with respect to Poincaré pairing unless
i+j\equiv 0\,(\mathrm{mod}\,N)
: for
a
in the
\xi^{i}
-eigenspace of
H^{k}_{c}(\tilde{U})
and
b
in the
\xi^{j}
-eigenspace of
H^{2n-k}(\tilde{U})
then

\xi^{i}\int_{\tilde{U}}a\wedge b=\int_{\tilde{U}}\sigma^{*}a\wedge b=\int_{\tilde{U}}a\wedge(\sigma^{-1})^{*}b=\xi^{-j}\int_{\tilde{U}}a\wedge b,

which means
\int_{\tilde{U}}a\wedge b
is zero unless
i+j\equiv 0\,(\mathrm{mod}\,N)
. Hence, the
\xi^{i}
-eigenspace of
H^{k}_{c}(\tilde{U})
is Poincaré dual to the
\xi^{-i}
-eigenspace of
H^{2n-k}(\tilde{U})
. On the other hand, since the
\xi^{i}
-eigenspace is complex conjugate to the
\xi^{-i}
-eigenspace, the
\xi^{i}
-eigenspace of
H^{k}_{c}(\tilde{U})
and the
\xi^{i}
-eigenspace of
H^{2n-k}(\tilde{U})
have the same dimension. We deduce that the morphism (
8.44
) must be an isomorphism.

We have
\tilde{L}=\eta^{*}L
on
H^{\bullet}_{c}(\tilde{U})
, because
\Theta
is supported on
E
. Therefore,

{\mathsf{X}}^{k}:{\mathbf{H}}^{n-k}\left(Z,\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\right)\to{\mathbf{H}}^{n+k}\left(Z,\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\right)(k)

is an isomorphism. We conclude
(1)
. It also follows that
\eta^{*}
identifies the primitive part of
{\mathsf{X}}

{\mathbf{H}}^{n-k}_{{\mathrm{p}}{\mathrm{r}}{\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{m}\,}}\left(Z,\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\right)

with the primitive part of
\tilde{\mathsf{X}}

\ker\left({\tilde{\mathsf{X}}}^{k+1}:{\mathbf{H}}^{n-k}\left(Z,\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\right)\to{\mathbf{H}}^{n+k+2}\left(Z,\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\right)\right).

Thus,
{\mathbf{H}}^{n-k}_{{\mathrm{p}}{\mathrm{r}}{\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{m}\,}}\left(Z,\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\right)
is a sub-Hodge structure of
H^{n-k}_{{\mathrm{p}}{\mathrm{r}}{\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{m}\,}}(\tilde{Z})
