. As the restriction of the polarization to a sub-Hodge structure still a polarization. We have proved
(2)
.
∎

The above two lemmas indicate that the graded vector space

\bigoplus_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbf{H}}^{k}\left(Z,\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\right)

is a polarized sub-Hodge-Lefschetz structure of
\bigoplus_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}H^{k}(\tilde{Z},{\mathbb{C}})
. In practice, it is more convenient to make the polarization independent of the resolution of singularities and intrinsic on
Z
. Heuristically, the local system
\mathbb{V}^{-j}
over
U
inherits a pairing from
{\mathbb{C}}_{\tilde{U}}
and it has a Hodge theoretic extension on its canonical extension. First, we can resolve
\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})
using
{\mathcal{A}}^{\bullet}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})
, the complex of
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}
-forms with log poles along
D^{(j)}
. Note that we have the inclusion of sheaves

{\mathcal{A}}^{n+k}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\wedge\overline{{\mathcal{A}}^{n-k}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}^{2n}_{Z}\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}(D^{(j)})\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{{\mathbb{C}}}}\overline{{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}(D^{(j)})}.

Since
{\mathcal{L}}^{N}\simeq{\mathscr{O}}_{Z}(D)
, picking local section of
l
such that
l^{N}=\prod_{i}x^{-\nu_{i}}_{i}
we can put a canonical singular Hermitian metric on
{\mathcal{L}}
by setting the weight function as

|l|_{h}=\prod_{i}|x_{i}|^{{-\nu_{i}}/{N}},\quad\text{where }x_{i}\text{ is the local defining equation of }D_{i}.

The induced singular Hermitian metric on
{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}(D^{(j)})={\mathcal{L}}^{-j}(\lfloor\frac{jD}{N}\rfloor+D^{(j)})
locally is

\left|l^{-j}\prod_{i}x^{-\lfloor j\mu_{i}/N\rfloor}_{i}\prod_{j\nu_{i}/N\notin{\mathbb{Z}}}x^{-1}_{i}\right|_{h}=\prod_{i}|x_{i}|^{{j\nu_{i}}/{N}-\lfloor{j\nu_{i}}/{N}\rfloor}\prod_{j\nu_{i}/N\notin{\mathbb{Z}}}|x_{i}|^{-1}=\prod_{i}|x_{i}|^{-\{-j\nu_{i}/N\}}.

For any smooth top form
\Upsilon
with values in
{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}(D^{(j)})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes\overline{{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}(D^{(j)})}
we can associate an integrable top form
(\Upsilon)_{h}=f\overline{g}|s|^{2}_{h}\,\mathrm{vol}(Z)
by fixing a volume form
\mathrm{vol}(Z)
on
Z
and writing locally
\Upsilon=fs\otimes\overline{gs}\,\mathrm{vol}(Z)
for
s
a local frame of
{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}(D^{(j)})
. Therefore, we obtain a well-defined pairing,

(8.45)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{k}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}\wedge\overline{{\mathcal{A}}^{k}_{Z}(\log D^{(j)})\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}^{(j)}}^{-1}}\to{\mathbb{C}},

\displaystyle(m^{\prime},\overline{m^{\prime\prime}})\mapsto\frac{\varepsilon(k+1)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{n}}\int_{Z}\left({\mathsf{X}}^{n-k}m^{\prime}\wedge\overline{m^{\prime\prime}}\right)_{h}.

Since
\eta:\tilde{Z}\to Z
is generic finite, we deduce from

\int_{\tilde{Z}}\eta^{*}\left({\mathsf{X}}^{n-k}m^{\prime}\wedge\overline{m^{\prime\prime}}\right)=N\int_{Z}\left({\mathsf{X}}^{n-k}m^{\prime}\wedge\overline{m^{\prime\prime}}\right)_{h}

that (
8.45
) induces the same polarization in the statement
(2)
of the above lemma except for the constant
N
.

Back to our situation, we obtain that
{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J}(E^{\alpha,J})
carries a canonical singular Hermitian metric
|-|_{h}
with local weight functions
\prod_{j\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}|z_{j}|^{-\{\alpha e_{j}\}}
restricted on
Y^{J}
, where
z_{i}
is the defining equation of
Y_{i}
. Provided the above two lemmas, the graded vector space

\bigoplus_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbf{H}}^{k}\left(Y^{J},\Omega_{Y^{J}}^{\bullet+\dim Y^{J}}(\log E^{\alpha,J})\otimes{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J}\right)

is a polarized Hodge-Lefschetz structure of central weight
\dim Y^{J}
for any non-empty subset
J
of
I_{\alpha}
. Similarly to Example
2.7
this is also determined by the filtered
{\mathscr{D}}_{Y^{J}}
-module
({\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J},F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J})
with the sesquilinear pairing

S_{\alpha,J}:{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}}\to\mathfrak{C}_{Y^{J}}
is given by

(8.46)

\left(\left[s_{1}\otimes P_{1}\right],\overline{\left[s_{2}\otimes P_{2}\right]}\right)\mapsto\frac{\varepsilon(\dim Y^{J}+1)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{\dim Y^{J}}}\int_{Y^{J}}(P_{1}\overline{P_{2}}-)\left(s_{1}\wedge\overline{s_{2}}\right)_{h}\

for local sections of
{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}
(see (
8.41
)) represented by
s_{i}\otimes P_{i}
such that
s_{i}
local sections of

\omega_{Y^{J}}(\log E^{\alpha,J})\otimes{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J}=\omega_{Y^{J}}\otimes{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J}(E^{\alpha,J})

and
P_{i}
is a differential operator
i=1,2
. Here,
(s_{1}\wedge\overline{s_{2}})_{h}
is the top form induced by the singular Hermitian metric on
{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,J}(E^{\alpha,J})
. Summarizing the results we have proved in this subsection:

Corollary 8.4
.

In the given notation, the graded vector space

\bigoplus_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}\left(Y^{J},{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Y^{J}}{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}\right)

underlies a polarized Hodge-Lefschetz structure of central weight
\dim Y^{J}
with the Hodge filtration induced by the sub complexes
F_{\bullet}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Y^{J}}{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}
and the polarization given by
\bigoplus_{k}S_{k}=\bigoplus_{k}\varepsilon(k)S_{k}^{\prime}
, where

\displaystyle S_{k}^{\prime}\colon={\mathbf{H}}^{k}(Y^{J},{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Y^{J}}{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J})\otimes\overline{{\mathbf{H}}^{-k}(Y^{J},{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Y^{J}}{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J})}

\displaystyle\to{\mathbf{H}}^{0}(Y^{J},{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Y^{J},\overline{Y^{J}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{S_{\alpha,J}}{\mathbf{H}}^{0}(Y^{J},{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{Y^{J},\overline{Y^{J}}}\mathfrak{C}_{Y^{J}})\simeq{\mathbb{C}}.

Remark 8.5
.

We cannot make the Hodge structure in the above corollary over
{\mathbb{Q}}
because there is no eigenvalue decomposition of
{\mathbb{Q}}
-structure.

8.2.
Sesquilinear pairing

As in the reduced case, we need a sesquilinear pairing to construct the limiting mixed Hodge structure. The construction for the reduced case still works with a little modification. Note that for any test function
\eta
over a local chart
U
and two local sections
\zeta_{1}\otimes P_{1},\zeta_{2}\otimes P_{2}\in{\mathbf{H}}^{0}\left(U,\Omega^{n}_{X/\Delta}(\log Y)(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\right)
, the function

t\mapsto\frac{\varepsilon(n+1)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{n}}\int_{X_{t}}P_{1}\overline{P_{2}}(\eta)\zeta_{1}\wedge\overline{\zeta_{2}}.

may have order approximately at most
{|t|^{2\alpha}\left(-\log|t^{2}|\right)^{k}}
near
t=0
where
k+1
is the number of components of
Y_{I_{\alpha}}
that intersect in
U
. This suggests that we can define the pairing
S_{\alpha}
on
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
by

\displaystyle\langle S_{\alpha}([\zeta_{1}\otimes P_{1}],\overline{[\zeta_{2}\otimes P_{2}]}),\eta\rangle

\displaystyle\colon={\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{s=-\alpha}\frac{\varepsilon(n+2)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{n+1}}\int_{X}|t|^{2s}P_{1}\overline{P_{2}}(\eta)\frac{dt}{t}\wedge\zeta_{1}\wedge\overline{\frac{dt}{t}\wedge\zeta_{2}}

\displaystyle={\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{s=-\alpha}\frac{\varepsilon(2)}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{\Delta}|t|^{2s}\frac{dt}{t}\wedge\overline{\frac{dt}{t}}\left(\frac{\varepsilon(n+1)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{n}}\int_{X_{t}}P_{1}\overline{P_{2}}(\eta)\zeta_{1}\wedge\overline{\zeta_{2}}\right).

We have not yet shown that
S_{\alpha}
is well-defined but let us perform some local calculations to gain some heuristic understanding.

Example 8.6
.

Suppose
Y=2Y_{0}
for
Y_{0}
is a smooth manifold and
t
is equal to
z^{2}_{0}
on
X
. Then
R
satisfies the equation
R(R-\frac{1}{2})=0
. We deduce that
{\mathcal{M}}
has two eigenspaces
{\mathcal{M}}_{0}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{\frac{1}{2}}
by (
4.18
). Then for any local sections
\zeta_{i}\otimes P_{i}=dz_{1}\wedge dz_{2}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}\otimes P_{i}
of
\Omega^{n}_{X/\Delta}(\log Y)\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
,
i=1,2
representing classes of
{\mathcal{M}}_{0}
, the calculation of the pairing
S_{0}([\zeta_{1}\otimes P_{1}],\overline{[\zeta_{2}\otimes P_{2}]})
is exactly as in the reduced case and as it turned out

S_{0}([\zeta_{1}\otimes P_{1}],\overline{[\zeta_{2}\otimes P_{2}]})={i_{Y_{0}}}_{+}S_{Y_{0}}\left([\zeta_{1}\otimes P_{1}],\overline{[\zeta_{2}\otimes P_{2}]}\right).

By Theorem
7.3

{\mathcal{M}}_{\frac{1}{2}}
is locally generated by the class represented by
dz_{1}\wedge dz_{2}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}\otimes z_{0}
. Now for any local sections
\zeta\otimes z_{0}P_{i}=dz_{1}\wedge dz_{2}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}\otimes z_{0}P_{i}
representing classes of
{\mathcal{M}}_{\frac{1}{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle S_{\frac{1}{2}}([\zeta\otimes z_{0}P_{1}],\overline{[\zeta\otimes z_{0}P_{2}]}),\eta\rangle

\displaystyle={\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{s=-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{X}|z_{0}|^{4s}P_{1}\overline{P_{2}}(\eta)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}\left(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{X}\frac{1}{2}\log|z_{0}|^{2}\partial_{0}\overline{\partial_{0}}P_{1}\overline{P_{2}}(\eta)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}\left(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{Y_{0}}\frac{1}{2}P_{1}\overline{P_{2}}(\eta)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=1}\left(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle\text{by Poincar\'{e}-Lelong equation~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GH}{}{}, Page 388]}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\langle{i_{Y_{0}}}_{+}S_{Y_{0}}(\left[\zeta_{1}\otimes P_{1}\right],\overline{\left[\zeta_{2}\otimes P_{2}\right]}),\eta\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\langle{i_{Y_{0}}}_{+}S_{\frac{1}{2},\{0\}}(\left[\zeta_{1}\otimes z_{0}P_{1}\right],\overline{\left[\zeta_{2}\otimes z_{0}P_{2}\right]}),\eta\rangle,

Recall
S_{\frac{1}{2},\{0\}}
defined in (
8.46
): since we have the isomorphism
{\mathscr{O}}_{Y_{0}}(2Y_{0})={\mathscr{O}}_{Y_{0}}(Y)\simeq{\mathscr{O}}_{Y_{0}}
there exists a canonical singular Hermitian metric (this case is smooth)
|-|_{h}
on
{\mathscr{O}}_{Y_{0}}(-Y_{0})
by setting the local frame
{z_{0}}
has norm
1
so that

\displaystyle{\displaystyle i_{Y_{0}}}_{+}S_{\frac{1}{2},\{0\}}(\left[\zeta_{1}\otimes z_{0}P_{1}\right],\overline{\left[\zeta_{2}\otimes z_{0}P_{2}\right])},\eta\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{X}|z_{0}|^{2}_{h}P_{1}\overline{P_{2}}(\eta)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}\left(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle={i_{Y_{0}}}_{+}S_{Y_{0}}(\left[\zeta_{1}\otimes P_{1}\right],\overline{\left[\zeta_{2}\otimes P_{2}\right]}),\eta\rangle.

The above equality can also be explained as follows: the cyclic covering constructed by taking out of the second root of the constant section of
{\mathscr{O}}_{Y_{0}}(2Y_{0})\simeq{\mathscr{O}}_{Y_{0}}
has two connected components and each component is isomorphic to
Y_{0}
.

Let
\eta
be a test function over an open subset
U
. For any two sections
m_{1},m_{2}\in H^{0}(U,\Omega^{n}_{X/\Delta}(\log Y)(-\lceil\alpha Y\rceil)\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X})
, the
(2n+2)
-form
\frac{dt}{t}\wedge m_{1}\wedge\overline{\frac{dt}{t}\wedge m_{2}}
is smooth of outside
Y
and has pole along
Y
. Locally, the
(2n+2)
-form just is
|z_{I}|^{2\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}P_{1}\overline{P_{2}}(\eta)\frac{dt}{t}\wedge\zeta\wedge\overline{\frac{dt}{t}\wedge\zeta}
, where
m_{j}=\zeta\otimes z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}P_{j}
for
\zeta=\frac{dz_{1}}{z_{1}}\wedge\frac{dz_{2}}{z_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\frac{dz_{k}}{z_{k}}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}
and
j=1,2
. Let
F(s)=F(s,m_{1},m_{2},\eta)
be the meromorphic extension of

\frac{\varepsilon(n+2)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{n+1}}\int_{X}|t|^{2s}\frac{dt}{t}\wedge m_{1}\wedge\overline{\frac{dt}{t}\wedge m_{2}}(\eta)

via integration by parts. The function
F(s)
is well defined when
\mathrm{Re\,}s>-\alpha
and has a pole at
s=-\alpha
. We only care about the polar part of
F(s)
at
s=-\alpha
.

Theorem 8.7
.

The polar part of
F(s)
at
s=-\alpha
only depends on the classes of
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
in
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
.

Proof.

Let
\{\rho_{\lambda}\}
be a partition of unity of the open covering
\{U_{\lambda}\}
by local charts. Then

F(s)=\sum_{\lambda}\frac{\varepsilon(n+2)}{\left(2\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)^{n+1}}\int_{U_{\lambda}}|t|^{2s}\frac{dt}{t}\wedge m_{1}\wedge\overline{\frac{dt}{t}\wedge m_{2}}(\rho_{\lambda}\eta).

Since
\rho_{\lambda}\eta
is a test function over local chart
U_{\lambda}
, we can assume
U
itself is a local chart. We assume
k+1
components of
Y
intersect in
U
.

Lemma 8.8
.

Under the assumption that
m_{i}=\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes P_{i}
for
\zeta_{\alpha}=z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}\frac{dz_{1}}{z_{1}}\wedge\frac{dz_{2}}{z_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\frac{dz_{k}}{z_{k}}\wedge dz_{k+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}
and for
i=1,2
, the followings are valid.

(1)

the order of the pole of
F(s)
at
s=-\alpha
is at most
k+1
;

(2)

if
P_{i}=t_{\alpha}P^{\prime}_{i}
for one of
i=1,2
, then
F(s)
is holomorphic at
s=-\alpha
;

(3)

for
0\leq j\leq k
we have,

\displaystyle F\left(s,\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes P_{1},\overline{\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes\frac{1}{e_{j}}z_{j}\partial_{j}P_{2}},\eta\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F\left(s,\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes\frac{1}{e_{j}}z_{j}\partial_{j}P_{1},\overline{\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes P_{2}},\eta\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(s+\frac{\lceil\alpha e_{j}\rceil}{e_{j}}\right)F(s,\zeta_{1}\otimes P_{1},\overline{\zeta_{2}\otimes P_{2}},\eta).

Proof of the lemma.

We work out Laurent series of
F(s)
at
s=-\alpha
:

\displaystyle F(s)

\displaystyle=\int_{X}|z_{I}|^{2s\mathbf{e}+2\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil-2\cdot\mathbf{1}}P_{1}\overline{P_{2}}(\eta)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}})

\displaystyle=\int_{X}|z_{I}|^{2(s+\alpha)\mathbf{e}-2\cdot\mathbf{1}}|z_{I}|^{2\{-\alpha\mathbf{e}\}}P_{1}\overline{P_{2}}(\eta)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}})

\displaystyle=\int_{X}(s+\alpha)^{-2(k+1)}|z_{I}|^{2(s+\alpha)\mathbf{e}}\eta^{\prime}\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}})

\displaystyle=\sum^{\infty}_{\ell=0}\frac{1}{\ell!}(s+\alpha)^{\ell-2(k+1)}\int_{X}(\log|z_{I}|^{2\mathbf{e}})^{\ell}\eta^{\prime}\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}}),

where
\eta^{\prime}\colon={\left(\prod_{i\in I}e_{i}^{-2}\right)\partial_{I}\overline{\partial_{I}}\left(|z_{I}|^{2\{-\alpha\mathbf{e}\}}P_{1}\overline{P_{2}}\eta\right)}
When
\ell<k+1
, then the form

(\log|z_{I}|^{2\mathbf{e}})^{\ell}\eta^{\prime}\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}}).

is actually exact because one of the
a_{i}
must be zero in the expansion of
(\log|z_{I}|^{2\mathbf{e}})^{\ell}
into the linear combination of
\prod^{k}_{i=0}\left(\log|z_{i}|^{2e_{i}}\right)^{a_{i}}
such that
\sum^{k}_{i=0}{a_{i}}=\ell
. Therefore, the order of the pole at
s=-\alpha
is at most
k+1
.

When
P_{1}=t_{\alpha}P^{\prime}_{1}
, the form

|z_{I}|^{2(s+\alpha)\mathbf{e}-2\cdot\mathbf{1}}|z_{I}|^{2\{-\alpha\mathbf{e}\}}t_{\alpha}P^{\prime}_{1}\overline{P_{2}}(\eta)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}})

is integrable when
s=-\alpha
where
\{-\alpha\mathbf{e}\}
is the multi-index such that
\{-\alpha\mathbf{e}\}_{i}=\{-\alpha e_{i}\}
. Therefore,
F(s)
is holomorphic at
s=-\alpha
. It is the same when
P_{2}=t_{\alpha}P^{\prime}_{2}
.

Lastly, by linearity, we can assume that
P_{1}=P_{2}=1
.

(8.47)

\begin{split}&F(s,\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes 1,\overline{\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes\frac{1}{e_{j}}z_{j}\partial_{j}},\eta)\\
=&\frac{\varepsilon(n+2)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{n+1}}\int_{X}|t|^{2s}\left(\frac{1}{e_{j}}\overline{z_{j}\partial_{j}}\eta\right)\frac{dt}{t}\wedge\zeta_{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\frac{dt}{t}\wedge\zeta_{\alpha}}\\
=&\int_{X}\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{j\}}|z_{i}|^{2se_{i}+2\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil-2}z^{se_{j}+\lceil\alpha e_{j}\rceil-1}_{j}\frac{1}{e_{j}}\overline{z^{se_{j}+\lceil\alpha e_{j}\rceil}_{j}\partial_{j}}\eta\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge{d\overline{z_{i}}})\\
=&\int_{X}-\left(s+\frac{\lceil\alpha e_{j}\rceil}{e_{j}}\right)\prod_{i\in I}|z_{i}|^{2se_{i}+2\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil-2}\eta\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}})\\
=&-\left(s+\frac{\lceil\alpha e_{j}\rceil}{e_{j}}\right)\frac{\varepsilon(n+2)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{n+1}}\int_{X}|t|^{2s}\eta\frac{dt}{t}\wedge\zeta_{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\frac{dt}{t}\wedge\zeta_{\alpha}}.\\
=&-\left(s+\frac{\lceil\alpha e_{j}\rceil}{e_{j}}\right)F(s,\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes 1,\overline{\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes 1},\eta).\end{split}

The other equality in
(3)
holds similarly.
∎

Returning to the proof of the theorem. Since
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
is locally represented by

\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}/(t_{\alpha},D_{1}+\alpha_{1},D_{2}+\alpha_{2},\dots,D_{n}+\alpha_{n}){\mathscr{D}}_{X}

(see the proof of Theorem
7.2
), and
(2)
and
(3)
in the lemma say that when one of
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
is in

\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes(t_{\alpha},D_{1}+\alpha_{1},D_{2}+\alpha_{2},\dots,D_{n}+\alpha_{n}){\mathscr{D}}_{X}

then
F(s)
is holomorphic since
\alpha_{i}
equals
\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil/e_{i}-\lceil\alpha e_{0}\rceil/e_{0}
for
1\leq i\leq k
and equals zero otherwise.
∎

For two sections
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in H^{0}(U,{\mathcal{M}})
and any test function
\eta
over
U
, we define the pairing
S_{\alpha}:{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}}\to\mathfrak{C}_{X}
by

\langle S_{\alpha}(\gamma_{1},\overline{\gamma_{2}}),\eta\rangle={\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{s=-\alpha}\sum_{\lambda}F(s,\tilde{\gamma}_{1},\overline{\tilde{\gamma}_{2}},\rho_{\lambda}\eta),

where
\{\rho_{\lambda}\}
is a partition of unity with respect to an open covering by local charts
\{U_{\lambda}\}
such that
\gamma_{i}
has a local lifting of
\tilde{\gamma}_{i}
over
U_{\lambda}
for
i=1,2
. It is obvious that
S_{\alpha}
is
{\mathscr{D}}_{X,\overline{X}}
-linear. Thus, it is a sesquilinear pairing.
As a corollary of Lemma
8.8
, we have

Corollary 8.9
.

We have
S_{\alpha}\circ({\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{d}\,}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{R_{\alpha}})=S_{\alpha}\circ({R_{\alpha}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{d}\,})
.

Because of the corollary, the sesquilinear pairing
S_{\alpha}
induces pairings on the associated graded quotient of the weight filtration

S_{\alpha}:{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{k}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-k}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}}\to\mathfrak{C}_{X},

as well as on the primitive part

P_{R_{\alpha}}S_{r}=S_{\alpha}\circ\left({\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{d}\,}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{R^{r}_{\alpha}}\right):{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r}}\to\mathfrak{C}_{X}.

Theorem 8.10
.

The isomorphism
\phi_{\alpha,r}:({\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r},F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r})\to{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,r}(-r)
in Theorem
7.13
respects the sesquilinear pairings up to a constant scalar. More concretely,

P_{R_{\alpha}}S_{r}(m_{1},\overline{m_{2}})=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset I_{\alpha},\\
|J|=r+1\end{subarray}}\frac{(-1)^{r}}{(r+1)!C_{J}}\tau^{J}_{+}S_{\alpha,J}(\phi_{\alpha,r}m_{1},\overline{\phi_{\alpha,r}m_{2}})

for any local sections
m_{1},m_{2}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r}
and
C_{J}=\prod_{j\in J}e_{j}
, where the pairing
S_{\alpha,J}:{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha,J}}\to\mathfrak{C}_{Y^{J}}
is defined in (
8.46
).

Proof.

Because the generators of
{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha,r}
are all monomials dividing
t_{\alpha}
of degree
\mu-r
by Corollary
7.7
, it suffices to prove the theorem in the case when
m_{i}
is represented by

\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes z_{{K_{i}}}=z^{\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}_{I}\frac{dz_{1}}{z_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\frac{dz_{k}}{z_{k}}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}\otimes z_{K_{i}}\quad

where
K_{i}\subset I_{\alpha}
with
|K_{i}|=\mu-r
and
i=1,2
. Let
\eta
be a test function over
U
. Then we have

\langle S_{\alpha}(m_{1},\overline{R^{r}_{\alpha}m_{2}}),\eta\rangle={\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{s=-\alpha}(-(s+\alpha))^{r}\int_{X}|z_{I}|^{2s\mathbf{e}+2\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil-2\cdot\mathbf{1}}z_{K_{1}}\overline{z_{K_{2}}}\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge\overline{dz_{i}}).

If
m_{1}\neq m_{2}
, then the above is zero. Indeed, for
v\in K_{2}\setminus K_{1}
we can make
R^{r}_{\alpha}
be the left multiplication by
1\otimes\prod_{i\in I\setminus K_{1}\setminus\{v\}}\frac{1}{e_{i}}z_{i}\partial_{i}
and then

\langle S(R^{r}_{\alpha}m_{1},\overline{m_{2}}),\eta\rangle={\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{s=-\alpha}\int_{X}|z_{I}|^{2s\mathbf{e}-2\mathbf{\cdot}\mathbf{1}}|z_{I}|^{2\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}\frac{t_{\alpha}}{z_{v}}\overline{z_{v}}\tilde{\eta}\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}})

where
\tilde{\eta}=C^{-1}_{I\setminus K_{1}\setminus\{v\}}\partial_{I\setminus K_{1}\setminus\{v\}}{\overline{z_{K_{2}}}}{\left(\overline{z_{v}}\right)^{-1}}\eta
is a smooth function with compact support. The function

\int_{X}|z_{I}|^{2s\mathbf{e}-2\mathbf{\cdot}\mathbf{1}}|z_{I}|^{2\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}\frac{t_{\alpha}}{z_{v}}\overline{z_{v}}\tilde{\eta}\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}})

is holomorphic at
s=-\alpha
because by setting
s=-\alpha
the form

|z_{I}|^{-2\alpha\mathbf{e}-2\mathbf{\cdot}\mathbf{1}}|z_{I}|^{2\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}\rceil}\frac{t_{\alpha}}{z_{v}}\overline{z_{v}}\tilde{\eta}\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}})=|z_{I\setminus I_{\alpha}}|^{-2\{\alpha\mathbf{e}\}}\frac{1}{\overline{t_{\alpha}}}\frac{\overline{z}_{v}}{z_{v}}\tilde{\eta}\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}})

is integrable.

Therefore, we reduce the proof to the case when
m_{1}=m_{2}=m
represented by
\zeta_{\alpha}\otimes z_{K}
. We shall prove that

S_{\alpha}(m,\overline{R^{r}_{\alpha}m})=\frac{(-1)^{r}}{(r+1)!C_{\overline{K}}}\tau^{\overline{K}}_{+}S_{\alpha,\overline{K}}(\phi_{\alpha,r}m,\overline{\phi_{\alpha,r}m}),

where
\overline{K}
is the complement of
K
in
I_{\alpha}
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
K=\{r+1,r+2,\dots,\mu\}
and
\overline{K}=\{0,1,\dots,r\}
so that
z_{K}=z_{r+1}z_{r+2}\cdots z_{\mu}
. We have

(8.48)

\langle S(m,\overline{R^{r}_{\alpha}m}),\eta\rangle={\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{s=-\alpha}g(s)

if we put

g(s)\colon=(-(s+\alpha))^{r}\int_{X}|z_{K}|^{2(s+\alpha)\mathbf{e}_{K}}|z_{I\setminus K}|^{2s\mathbf{e}_{I\setminus K}+2\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}_{I\setminus K}\rceil-2}\eta\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}})

where, for any index subset
J\subset I
, the
j
-th component of
\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}_{J}\rceil
is
\lceil\alpha e_{j}\rceil
if
j\in J
or zero otherwise.
Integration by parts for
\{dz_{i},d\bar{z}_{i}\}_{i\in\overline{K}}
,

\displaystyle g(s)=\int_{X}\frac{|z_{I_{\alpha}}|^{2(s+\alpha)\mathbf{e}_{I_{\alpha}}}}{C^{2}_{\overline{K}}(s+\alpha)^{2r+2}}|z_{I\setminus I_{\alpha}}|^{2s\mathbf{e}_{I\setminus I_{\alpha}}+2\lceil\alpha\mathbf{e}_{I\setminus I_{\alpha}}\rceil-2}\left(\partial_{\overline{K}}\overline{\partial_{\overline{K}}}\eta\right)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}})

(8.49)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{r}}{C^{2}_{\overline{K}}(s+\alpha)^{r+2}}\int_{X}{|t|^{2(s+\alpha)}}\prod_{j\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}|z_{j}|^{-2\{\alpha e_{j}\}}\left(\partial_{\overline{K}}\overline{\partial_{\overline{K}}}\eta\right)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}}),

where
\partial_{\overline{K}}\overline{\partial_{\overline{K}}}=\prod_{j\in\overline{K}}\partial_{j}\overline{\partial_{j}}
. Because of the expansion

|t|^{2(s+\alpha)}=\exp\left({\log|t|^{2}\left(s+\alpha\right)}\right)=\sum^{\infty}_{\ell=0}\frac{\left(\log|t|^{2}\right)^{\ell}(s+\alpha)^{\ell}}{\ell!},

we find that by (
8.49
)

(8.50)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{s=-\alpha}g(s)=

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{r}}{C^{2}_{\overline{K}}(r+1)!}\int_{X}{\left(\log|t|^{2}\right)^{r+1}}\prod_{j\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}|z_{j}|^{-2\{\alpha e_{j}\}}\left(\partial_{\overline{K}}\overline{\partial_{\overline{K}}}\eta\right)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}})

The expansion of
\left(\log|t|^{2}\right)^{r+1}
is a linear combination of

\prod_{i\in I}\left(\log|z_{i}|^{2}\right)^{a_{i}}

for all partitions
\sum_{i\in I}a_{i}=r+1
, but the differential form

\prod_{i\in I}\left(\log|z_{i}|^{2}\right)^{a_{i}}\prod_{j\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}|z_{j}|^{-2\{\alpha e_{j}\}}\left(\partial_{\overline{K}}\overline{\partial_{\overline{K}}}\eta\right)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}})

is exact unless
a_{i}\neq 0
for any
i\in\overline{K}
, which is equivalent to
a_{i}=1
for
i\in\overline{K}
and
a_{i}=0
for
i\notin\overline{K}
. It follows from (
8.50
) that
{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{s=-\alpha}g(s)
is equal to

\frac{(-1)^{r}}{C_{\overline{K}}(r+1)!}\int_{X}{\prod_{j\in\overline{K}}\log|z_{j}|^{2}}\prod_{j\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}|z_{j}|^{-2\{\alpha e_{j}\}}\left(\partial_{\overline{K}}\overline{\partial_{\overline{K}}}\eta\right)\bigwedge^{n}_{i=0}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}}).

An application of Poincáre-Lelong equation
[
GH14
, Page 388]
gives

(8.51)

{\mathrm{R}}{\mathrm{e}}{\mathrm{s}}_{s=-\alpha}g(s)=\frac{(-1)^{r}}{(r+1)!C_{\overline{K}}}\int_{Y^{\overline{K}}}\prod_{j\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}|z_{j}|^{-2\{\alpha e_{j}\}}\eta\bigwedge^{n}_{i=r+1}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}})

Since
\phi_{\alpha,\overline{K}}m=\pm\frac{dz_{I\setminus K}}{z_{I\setminus K}}\wedge dz_{k+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}\otimes s_{\alpha,\overline{K}}
is a local section of
\omega_{Y^{\overline{K}}}(E^{\alpha,\overline{K}})\otimes{\mathscr{V}}_{\alpha,\overline{K}}
, we deduce that

(\phi_{\alpha,\overline{K}}m\wedge\overline{\phi_{\alpha,\overline{K}}m})_{h}=\prod_{j\in I\setminus I_{\alpha}}|z_{j}|^{-2\{\alpha e_{j}\}}\bigwedge^{n}_{i=r+1}(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}dz_{i}\wedge d\overline{z_{i}})

from which we conclude that (
8.51
) is equal to

\frac{(-1)^{r}}{(r+1)!C_{\overline{K}}}\int_{Y^{\overline{K}}}\eta(\phi_{\alpha,\overline{K}}m\wedge\overline{\phi_{\alpha,\overline{K}}m})_{h}=\frac{(-1)^{r}}{(r+1)!C_{\overline{K}}}\langle S_{\alpha,\overline{K}}(\phi_{\alpha,\overline{K}}m,\phi_{\alpha,\overline{K}}m),\eta\rangle.

See (
8.46
). We have concluded the proof.
∎

8.3.
Construction of the limiting mixed Hodge structure

We begin to construct a polarized bigraded Hodge-Lefschetz structure on
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}{\mathbf{H}}^{\bullet}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha})
. Fix a Kähler class
\omega
on
X
and let
L=\omega\wedge:{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}\to{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}[2]
be the Lefschetz operator and
{\mathsf{X}}_{1}=2\pi\sqrt{-1}L
. Relabel the graded pieces of the first page of the weight spectral sequence by

V^{\alpha}_{\ell,k}={\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{k}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha})=\prescript{W}{}{E^{-k,\ell+k}_{1}}.

Let
V^{\alpha}=\bigoplus_{\ell,k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}V^{\alpha}_{\ell,k}
with the filtration
F_{\bullet}V^{\alpha}
induced by
F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
. Denote by
E_{i}(R_{\alpha})
the induced operator by
R_{\alpha}
on
\prescript{W}{}{E_{i}}
and let
{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}=E_{1}(R_{\alpha})
. Denote by
S_{\ell,k}=\varepsilon(\ell)S_{\ell,k}^{\prime}
the induced pairing on
V^{\alpha}_{\ell,k}\otimes\overline{V^{\alpha}_{-\ell,-k}}
with

\displaystyle S_{\ell,k}^{\prime}\colon

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{k}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha})\otimes\overline{{\mathbf{H}}^{-\ell}(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-k}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}})\to

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{H}}^{0}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X,\overline{X}}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{k}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-k}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}})\to{\mathbf{H}}^{0}_{c}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X,\overline{X}}\mathfrak{C}_{X})\simeq{\mathbb{C}}.

Let
d_{1}
be the differential of the first page of the spectral sequence. In terms of relabeling, we have

d_{1}:(V^{\alpha}_{\ell,k},F_{\bullet}V^{\alpha}_{\ell,k})\to(V^{\alpha}_{\ell+1,k-1},F_{\bullet}V^{\alpha}_{\ell+1,k-1}).

Exactly same to Theorem
6.6
and Corollary
6.7
in the reduced case, we conclude that

Theorem 8.11
.

The tuple
(V^{\alpha},{\mathsf{X}}_{1},{\mathsf{Y}}_{2},F_{\bullet}V,\bigoplus S_{j,k},d_{1})
gives a differential polarized bigraded Hodge-Lefschetz structure of central weight
n
.

Corollary 8.12
.

We have the following

(1)

the Hodge spectral sequence degenerates at
\prescript{}{F}{E_{1}}
;

(2)

the weight spectral sequence degenerates at
\prescript{W}{}{E_{2}}
;

(3)

the tuple
(\bigoplus_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}),{\mathsf{X}}_{1},{\mathsf{Y}}_{2},F_{\bullet})
together with the pairing induced by
S_{\alpha}
is a polarized bigraded Hodge-Lefschetz structure of central weight
n
.

The last statement in Corollary
8.12
implies that the monodromy filtration associated to
R_{\alpha}
on
{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha})
is the correct weight filtration. Thus, we have established Theorem
A
.

The following is an immediate consequence of the existence of the bigraded limiting Hodge-Lefschetz structure.

Corollary 8.13

(Hard Lefschetz)
.

The Lefschetz operator induces an isomorphism between
{\mathscr{O}}_{\Delta}
-modules

\left(2\pi\sqrt{-1}L\right)^{k}:F_{\ell}{\mathbf{R}}^{-k}f_{*}\Omega^{{}\bullet+n}_{X/\Delta}(\log Y)\simeq F_{\ell-k}{\mathbf{R}}^{k}f_{*}\Omega^{{}\bullet+n}_{X/\Delta}(\log Y)\quad\text{for any integer }\ell.

As a result, we have the following decomposition in the derived category of coherent
{\mathscr{O}}_{\Delta}
-modules:

{\mathbf{R}}f_{*}F_{\ell}\Omega^{{}\bullet+n}_{X/\Delta}(\log Y)\simeq\bigoplus_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}F_{\ell}{\mathbf{R}}^{k}f_{*}\Omega^{{}\bullet+n}_{X/\Delta}(\log Y)[-k]\quad\text{ for any integer }\ell.

Proof.

The first statement follows from the Hard Lefschetz on each fiber

\left(2\pi\sqrt{-1}L\right)^{k}:F_{\ell}{\mathbf{R}}^{-k}f_{*}\Omega^{{}\bullet+n}_{X/\Delta}(\log Y)\otimes{\mathbb{C}}(p)\simeq F_{\ell-k}{\mathbf{R}}^{k}f_{*}\Omega^{{}\bullet+n}_{X/\Delta}(\log Y)\otimes{\mathbb{C}}(p),

for every
p\in\Delta
. The second statement follows from the first one plus the main theorem in
[
Del68
]
.
∎

9.
Local invariant cycle theorem

Let
Z
be a complex a complex manifold of
\dim Z=n
and
D=\sum_{i\in K}D_{i}
be a simple normal crossing divisor where
D_{i}
’s are smooth divisors. Let
D_{J}=\sum_{j\in J}D_{j}
for some index set
J
. The log de Rham complex induced by the canonical morphism
{\mathscr{O}}_{Z}(-D_{J})\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}\to\Omega_{Z}(\log D_{J})(-D_{J})\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}
:

(9.52)

\Omega^{n+\bullet}_{Z}(\log D_{J})(-D_{J})\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}

is a filtered resolution of a holonomic
{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}
-module
\omega_{Z}(!D_{J})
, which can be proved using the idea in the proof of Lemma
7.9
. Indeed,
\omega_{Z}(!D_{J})
is the holonomic dual of
\omega_{Z}(*D_{J})
, by noticing that
\Omega^{k}_{Z}(\log D_{J})(-D_{J})\simeq{\mathbf{R}}{\mathcal{H}}{o}{m}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{Z}}(\Omega^{m-k}_{Z}(\log D_{J}),\omega_{Z})
but we will not use this fact. The short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\Omega^{n+\bullet}_{Z}(\log D_{J\cup\{k\}})(-D_{J\cup\{k\}})

\displaystyle\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}\to\Omega^{n+\bullet}_{Z}(\log D_{J})(-D_{J})\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}

\displaystyle\to\Omega^{n+\bullet}_{D_{k}}(\log D_{J}\cup{D_{k}})(-\log D_{J}\cup{D_{k}})\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}\to 0

indicates that, by taking the cohomology of the induced long exact sequence,

(9.53)

0\to\tau^{k}_{+}\omega_{D_{k}}(!D_{J}\cap{D_{k}})\to\omega_{Z}(!D_{J\cup\{k\}})\to\omega_{Z}(!D_{J})\to 0

is a filtered exact sequence. Here,
\tau^{k}:D_{k}\to Z
is the closed embedding.

We also need that the morphism of
\omega_{Z}(!D)\to\omega_{Z}(*D)
factors through
\omega_{Z}
because

\Omega_{Z}^{n+\bullet}(\log D)(-D)\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}\to\Omega_{Z}^{n+\bullet}(\log D)\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}

factors through
\Omega_{Z}^{n+\bullet}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}
. Since
\omega_{Z}
is simple on each connected component, we have

(9.54)

\omega_{Z}(!D)\twoheadrightarrow\omega_{Z}\hookrightarrow\omega_{Z}(*D).

Moreover, all morphisms strictly respect the good filtrations thanks to

F_{\ell}\omega_{Z}(!D)=F_{\ell}\omega_{Z}(*D)=F_{\ell}\omega_{Z}=0

for
\ell<-n
and

\underbrace{\Omega^{n}_{Z}(\log D)(-D)}_{=F_{-n}\omega_{Z}(!D)}\twoheadrightarrow\omega_{Z}\hookrightarrow\underbrace{\Omega_{Z}^{n}(\log D)}_{=F_{-n}\omega_{Z}(*D)}

by (
9.52
) and (
2.2
).

Now we present the proof of Theorem
B
, which is equivalent to the following statement, using the same notation introduced in §
7
:

Theorem 9.1
.

The following sequence is exact in the category of mixed Hodge structures:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\ell}(Y,{\mathbb{C}}[n])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}})(-1).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}

Proof.

Note that
{\ker}R
is contained in
{\mathcal{M}}_{0}
and
W_{-j}{\ker}R=R^{j}{\ker}R^{j+1}
for
j\geq 0
and is trivial for
j<0
where
W
is the filtration induced by
W=W(R)
on
{\mathcal{M}}_{0}
. It follows that
({\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-j}\ker R,F)\simeq(\omega_{\tilde{Y}^{(j+1)}},F)
for
j\geq 0
by Theorem
7.13
. Since
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-j}{\ker}R
is a summand of
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-j}{\mathcal{M}}_{0}
for
j\geq 0
according to the Lefschetz decomposition on
{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}{\mathcal{M}}_{0}
, we have the following short exact sequence of Hodge structures on the first page of the weight spectral sequences:

\displaystyle 0\to{{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell+\bullet}}(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-j-\bullet}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\ker}R)\to E_{1}^{j+\bullet,\ell-j}\xrightarrow{R}E_{1}^{j+2+\bullet,\ell-j}(-1)\to 0,

for
-j-\bullet\leq 1
where
E_{1}^{j+\bullet,\ell-j}={{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell+\bullet}}(X,{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-j-\bullet}{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}_{0})
is a Hodge structure of weight
n+\ell-j
. The associated long exact sequence gives the relation between the second page of the weight spectral sequences for
j\geq-1
:

\to{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-j}{{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\ker}R)\to{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-j}{{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}_{0})\xrightarrow{R}{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-j-2}{{\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{M}}_{0})(-1)\to.

Then the proof would be completed if we can show that there is canonical quasi-isomorphism
{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}\ker R\simeq{\mathbb{C}}_{Y}[n]
and

({\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}\ker R),F,W)\simeq({\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathbb{C}}_{Y}[n]),F,W)

as mixed Hodge structures.

We make use of the notation in the proof of Theorem
7.13
. Taking
\alpha=0
in (
7.39
) gives an exact sequence:

0\to{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\xrightarrow{R}{\mathcal{M}}_{0}(-1)\to{\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{0}(-1)\to 0,

which implies that
({\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0},F)\simeq(\ker R,F)
. Recall that
{\mathcal{H}}^{j}_{0}={\mathscr{H}}^{j}(\Omega_{X}^{\bullet+n+1}(\log Y)|_{Y_{\rm red}}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X})
. On the other hand, the filtered
{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
-modules
{\mathcal{H}}^{j}_{0}
also fit into the exact sequence of filtered
{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
-modules:

0\to{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}\to\omega_{X}(!Y)\to\omega(*Y)\to{\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{0}\to 0

because of (
9.52
), (
2.2
) and the short exact sequence

0\to\Omega_{X}^{\bullet+n}(\log Y)(-Y_{\rm Red})\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\to\Omega_{X}^{\bullet+n}(\log Y)\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\to\Omega_{X}^{\bullet+n}(\log Y)|_{Y_{\rm Red}}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\to 0.

As the image of
\omega_{X}(!Y)\to\omega(*Y)
is precisely
\omega_{X}
by (
9.54
), we obtain a filtered short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}\to\omega(!Y)\to\omega_{X}\to 0.

Now we describe a weight filtration on
\omega_{X}(!Y)
which also induces a weight filtration on
{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}
. Define the Čech complex

(K_{J},F)=\left\{(\omega(!Y_{J}),F)\xrightarrow{d^{\prime}}\bigoplus_{i\in J}(\omega_{X}(!Y_{J\setminus\{i\}}),F)\xrightarrow{d^{\prime}}\cdots\xrightarrow{d^{\prime}}(\omega_{X},F)\right\}

placed in degree
-|J|,\dots,-1,0
for any subset
J
of
I
, recalling that
Y_{J}=\bigcup_{j\in J}Y_{j}
. Indeed,
(K_{J},F)\simeq(\tau^{J}_{+}\omega_{Y^{J}},F)[|J|]
by (
9.53
) because
(K_{J},F)
is the iterated mapping cones (up to a shift) of the canonical maps
\omega_{X}(!Y_{i})\to\omega_{X}
. It follows that

(9.55)

({\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}K_{J},F)\simeq\left(\Omega^{n+1+\bullet}_{Y^{J}},F\right).

Consider the double complex

K^{\bullet,\bullet}=\left\{\omega_{X}\xrightarrow{d^{\prime\prime}}\bigoplus_{i\in I}K_{\{i\}}\xrightarrow{d^{\prime\prime}}\bigoplus_{i,j\in I}K_{\{i,j\}}\xrightarrow{d^{\prime\prime}}\cdots\xrightarrow{d^{\prime\prime}}K_{I}\right\}

where the
p
-th column
K^{\bullet,p}=\bigoplus_{|J|=p+1}K_{J}
if
p\geq 0
,
K^{\bullet,-1}=\omega_{X}
and the
q
-th row
K^{q,\bullet}
is given by iterating the mapping cone (up to a shift) of the identity map
\omega_{X}(!Y_{J})\to\omega_{X}(!Y_{J})
exactly
(|I|+q)
-many times (hence filtered acyclic):

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{|J|=-q}\left\{\omega_{X}(!Y_{J})\xrightarrow{d^{\prime\prime}}\bigoplus_{|I|+q}\omega_{X}(!Y_{J})\xrightarrow{d^{\prime\prime}}\cdots\xrightarrow{d^{\prime\prime}}\omega(!Y_{J})\right\}[q+1]\quad\text{for}\quad-|I|<q\leq 0

and
\omega_{X}(!Y)[-|I|+1]
for
(q=-|I|)
. It follows that the total complex
K\colon=\text{Tot}(K^{\bullet,\bullet})
is filtered quasi-isomorphic to
K^{-|I|,|I|-1}[1]=\omega_{X}(!Y)[1]
because all other the rows are filtered acyclic. We define the weight filtration by
W_{-j}K\colon=\text{Tot}(K^{\bullet,\geq j})
and it induces a weight filtration on
{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}(K)=\omega_{X}(!Y)
. It follows from the short exact sequence

0\to W_{0}K\to\underbrace{\omega_{X}(!Y)[1]}_{=W_{1}K}\to\underbrace{\omega_{X}[1]}_{={\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{1}K}\to 0

that
(W_{0}K,F)\simeq({\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}[1],F)
. Moreover, we see that
W_{-j}K
is supported on cohomological degree
1
for any
j\geq 0
by descending induction and the short exact sequence

0\to W_{-j-1}K\to W_{-j}K\to\underbrace{\tau^{(j+1)}_{+}\omega_{\tilde{Y}^{(j+1)}}[1]}_{={\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-j}K}\to 0.

Therefore,
(W_{-j}{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}[1],F)\simeq(W_{-j}K,F)
for
j\leq 0
.

A diagram chasing implies

(9.56)

W_{-j}{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}\simeq{\mathscr{H}}^{-1}(W_{-j}K)=\ker\left(\omega_{X}(!Y)\to\bigoplus_{J\subset I,|J|=j}\omega_{X}(!Y_{J})\right).

Indeed, since
d^{\prime\prime}\colon\omega_{X}(!Y_{J})\to\bigoplus_{|I|+q}\omega_{X}(!Y_{J})
is injective for any subset
J\subset I
with
|J|=-q
, for any element
m\in W_{-j}\omega_{X}(!Y)
, it has a unique lifting in
{\mathscr{H}}^{-1}(W_{-j}K)
represented by the cocycle

m_{-j}+m_{-j-1}+\cdots+m_{-|I|+1}

where
m_{\ell}\in K^{-\ell,\ell-1}
is the image of
m
under the morphism

\omega_{X}(!Y)\to\bigoplus_{J\subset I,|J|=-\ell+1}\omega_{X}(!Y_{J})

up to a sign (determined by the cocycle condition). The cocycle condition also imposes that
d^{\prime}m_{-j}=0\in K^{j,-j}=\bigoplus_{|J|=j+1}\bigoplus_{i\in J}\omega_{X}(!Y_{J\setminus\{i\}})
and hence, we have proved (
9.56
).

By definition
{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}W_{0}K
with the weight filtration
{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}W_{-j}K
for
j\geq 0
bifiltered quasi-isomorphic, by (
9.55
), to the total complex of

\left\{\bigoplus_{i\in I}\Omega^{n+1+\bullet}_{Y^{\{i\}}}\to\bigoplus_{i,j\in I}\Omega^{n+1+\bullet}_{Y^{\{i,j\}}}\to\cdots\to\Omega^{n+1+\bullet}_{Y^{I}}\right\}

with the standard filtrations computing the mixed Hodge structure on the cohomology of
{\mathbb{C}}_{Y}[n+1]
; see
[
GS75
, (4.2)]
or
[
Ste76
, (3.5)]
. More precisely, we have

({\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}),W,F)\simeq(H^{\ell}(Y,{\mathbb{C}}_{Y}[n]),F,W)

as mixed Hodge structures.

Lastly, we prove that the isomorphism
\ker R\to{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}
preserves the weight filtrations, i.e.,
W_{-j}\ker R\to W_{-j}{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}
is an isomorphism, which will imply that

({\mathbf{H}}^{\ell}(X,{\mathrm{D}}{\mathrm{R}}_{X}\ker R),F,W)\simeq(H^{\ell}(Y,{\mathbb{C}}_{Y}[n]),F,W)

as mixed Hodge structures. To prove this, it suffices to show that the image of
W_{-j}\ker R\to\omega_{X}(!Y)
lies in
W_{-j}{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}
because of descending induction: if we assume that
W_{-j}\ker R\to W_{-j}{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}
is an isomorphism then

{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-j}\ker R\to{\mathrm{g}}{\mathrm{r}}^{W}_{-j}{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}

is a surjection, which automatically implies it is an isomorphism because the characteristic cycles of the source and the target are the same. Thus,
W_{-j-1}\ker R\to W_{-j-1}{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}
is an isomorphism.

It remains to show that the image of
W_{-j}\ker R
is in
W_{-j}{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}
. We must show that
W_{-j}\ker R\to\omega_{X}(!Y)
is contained in
\ker\left(\omega_{X}(!Y)\to\omega_{X}(!Y_{J})\right)
for any subset
J\subset I
of cardinality
j
. Using a similar idea of the proof for Lemma
7.10
the complex of
{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
-modules
\Omega^{n+1+\bullet}_{X}(\log Y)(-Y_{J})\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
is a filtered resolution of a filtered
{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
-module
\omega_{X}(!Y_{J})^{*}
and
\omega_{X}(!Y_{J})\to\omega_{X}(!Y_{J})^{*}
is a strict inclusion. Therefore,

\ker(\omega_{X}(!Y)\to\omega_{X}(!Y_{J})^{*})=\ker(\omega_{X}(!Y)\to\omega_{X}(!Y_{J})).

The short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\Omega^{n+1+\bullet}_{X}(\log Y)(-Y)\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}

\displaystyle\to\Omega^{n+1+\bullet}_{X}(\log Y)(-Y_{J})\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}\to T^{\bullet}\to 0

where
T^{\bullet}\colon=\Omega^{n+1+\bullet}_{X}(\log Y)(-Y_{J})|_{Y_{I\setminus J}}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}_{X}
, implies that
\ker(\omega_{X}(!Y)\to\omega_{X}(!Y_{J})^{*})\simeq{\mathscr{H}}^{0}\left(T^{\bullet}\right)
. Therefore, it suffices to prove that
R^{j}\ker R^{j+1}
is contained in the image of

(9.57)

{\mathscr{H}}^{0}\left(T^{\bullet}\right)\to{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}_{0}\to{\mathcal{M}}.

We can verify this using local coordinates. For any local section
m\in R^{j}\ker R^{j+1}
, it can be locally represented by

m=\zeta_{0}\otimes z_{I\setminus\{a\}}\partial_{I\setminus\{a\}\setminus J}\cdot P

for some
a\notin J
. Then we consider the form

\frac{dz_{0}}{z_{0}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\hat{\frac{dz_{a}}{z_{a}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\frac{dz_{k}}{z_{k}}\wedge\cdots{dz_{n}}\otimes z_{I\setminus\{a\}}\partial_{I\setminus\{a\}\setminus J}\cdot P

where the hat denotes the omission of that term. This form defines a class in
{\mathscr{H}}^{0}\left(T^{\bullet}\right)
and its image under (
9.57
) is precisely
m
, which completes the proof.
∎
